# Security cleanup assessment

## Initial implementation baseline

- Recorded at repository commit `78812e4d0a2d4ff422e03cd71611d6eb4ab4c57d`; the worktree is dirty (16 staged paths and 6 unstaged paths, including existing user-owned docs and a product source edit). This is exploratory state, not a frozen release candidate. No existing changes were overwritten.
- Toolchain observed: Gradle 9.6.0, Kotlin 2.3.21, AGP 9.5.1, SDK compile 37 / target 35 / minimum 28, JDK 25 launcher with Gradle daemon criteria in `gradle/gradle-daemon-jvm.properties`. Android SDK `adb` is available.
- Settings declares 14 production modules. The hosted runner restriction in Feature 058 remains in force; this audit does not enable it.
- Earlier audit reported 19 host passes; these are historical and are not new verification for this implementation.
- The disposable `clipboardAudit` target/observer packages support isolated synthetic clipboard cases and were used on the approved Mi A2 API 29 cohort; both packages were uninstalled after each run. No isolated synthetic profile and controlled raw-heap artifact store has been established. A foreground scheduled-attempt case and an app-switch ownership-unavailable skip are recorded for that single API 29/OEM cohort. API 28, API 33-34, API 35+ physical cohorts, lock/sleep, preview/TalkBack, full heap and frozen-candidate evidence remain Unverified.

## Canonical classification and boundary reconciliation (partial)

On 2026-11-14, the current inventory reconciliation found seven FIDO cleanup usages with stale or invented register links. SQLDelight credential/consent row arrays had been linked to `CLS-FIDO-004` (paired Bluetooth host identifier), while CTAP response buffers used the same unrelated classification; these are now linked to RP/user association and credential-ID classifications (`CLS-FIDO-001`/`CLS-FIDO-002`). The temporary client-data hash and assertion signing buffers now use the new ceremony-scoped `CLS-FIDO-008`. Their `BND-FIDO-*`/`BND-CTAP-*` values were removed because these mutable byte-array lifecycle operations are not platform text boundaries; the canonical boundary register now states that scope explicitly. The affected inventory rows' persistence, logging, and terminal-event descriptions were corrected to match their actual owners. A later canonical-reference check removed the unregistered `BND-DB-001` links from five Vault persistence/serialization usages because database serialization is not a platform UI text boundary.

A structural reconciliation against the canonical Markdown registers now reports 19 classification IDs, 8 boundary IDs, zero missing usage classifications, zero missing usage boundary IDs, and zero remaining incorrect paired-host/`BND-FIDO`/`BND-CTAP` links among the seven corrected usages. A secondary semantic review also removed channel-ID classifications from unrelated PRF/paired-device rows, classified the HMAC extension's salt-length diagnostic under `CLS-FIDO-007`, and added the RP/user and KeyStore-alias classifications to the exception-message source and test rows. The configured diagnostic test now links its synthetic seed marker to `CLS-KEY-001`. SEC065-LOG-042 maps the locally derived RP credential-candidate count to `CLS-FIDO-002`; the canonical register now names `GetAssertionUseCase` and `Ctap2GetAssertionHandler` and prohibits logging those counts. This is documentation and link validation only: it does not establish source-owner completeness, runtime behavior, heap cleanup, or final-candidate reconciliation. T039 remains open for review and reconciliation of all other inventory usages and links.

While reviewing open inventory dispositions, I found that the production Android `EncryptionWrapper` and its two Android test source sets had no usage records, despite the wrapper's AndroidKeyStore-backed wallet-seed encryption path. Added one production and two test usage records, changed those file dispositions to Relevant, and updated `CLS-KEY-006` from the removed FIDO credential-storage service to the current `core:common` wrapper and its fixed KeyStore alias. The caller's plaintext cleanup is source-reviewed; these added tests were not executed in this continuation and cover only successful round trips. Failure injection, provider copies, and heap roots remain unverified; T005 and T015 stay open.

A second open-path review found the live `WalletMasterSeedProvider` and its owner-focused host suite had also remained Open in the inventory despite the 16-case suite and existing seed-session assessment. Added source/test usage rows for bootstrap buffers, session PRK/parent-key caches, returned borrower copies, metadata-key provisioning, mnemonic import consumption and joined-owner release. The current XML report shows 16 passed cases, including gated cancellation/replacement and cache cleanup; this source-to-test mapping does not prove provider erasure or heap-root absence.

The SEC065-CTAP-007 follow-up now exercises the sensitive decoder, nested extension map, real `HmacSecretProcessor`, and outer request cleanup in one deterministic host test. All five `HmacSecretProcessorZeroingTest` cases passed; the same decoded salt owner was readable during processing and zeroed afterward. Its test source now has an explicit usage row and refreshed source hash. This is host evidence, not a production `handleRaw` device run or heap inspection.

The usage-link reconciliation also found nine files already marked Relevant whose test-only synthetic cases had no explicit usage rows. Added test-specific usage records for BuildVariant exception capture, transport-preference diagnostics, Android BLE GATT diagnostics, HID lifecycle/privacy cases, transport-selection output, FIDO error-to-UI mapping, transport/camera/discoverability captures, LatencyProfiler identifiers and VaultItem buffer disposal. The current snapshot contains 725 file records and 631 usage records; every Relevant file has at least one usage, usage/file links reconcile in both directions, and every usage classification/boundary reference resolves. This structural reconciliation does not establish source-owner completeness or final-candidate status.

## Evidence-index format preflight (partial)

The exploratory `evidence/index.md` preflight found two diagnostic records joined into a single Markdown line and two report links pointing to the wrong Gradle output directory. The rows are now separated, and both links target the existing XML test-result artifacts. Revalidation finds 97 evidence rows with exactly ten columns each and zero missing repository-local artifact links. This checks table shape and current local path existence only; it does not verify the ten values' adequacy, hashes or reviewer access to controlled raw artifacts, candidate freshness, rejected-record behavior, or final T037 acceptance. The frozen-candidate validation remains open.

No task was marked complete based solely on the initial baseline. Since then, the source inventory and finding ledger have been populated and expanded through implementation review; both remain open for source-owner confirmation, complete diagnostic reachability and final-candidate reconciliation.

## Bounded seed-export finding

- **SEC065-SEED-001 — Corrected, focused host check passed (exploratory revision).** `DevToolsViewModel.CopyToClipboard` previously converted the revealed mnemonic `CharArray` to an immutable `String`, submitted it to the password clipboard service with an identifying label and 60-second delay, then claimed safe copying. This path has no approved seed-export boundary. The action now fails closed, reports “Mnemonic clipboard export is disabled.”, and does not create or submit a mnemonic copy. `DevToolsViewModelTest` verifies no clipboard call, the disabled message and continued erasure of the UI-owned words on hide. Regression failed before the correction and passed after it.
- Follow-up cleanup removed obsolete `clipboardCopyJob` bookkeeping because no mnemonic copy job can now be created. Focused `DevToolsViewModelTest` rerun passes 19 cases across two reports, and FIDO KtLint passes; the source inventory hash and evidence index are refreshed. This does not add device, heap or release-candidate evidence.
- The focused result does not establish heap absence, physical-device behavior, or release-candidate acceptance.

## Bounded legacy credential-copy finding

- **SEC065-COPY-001 — Unreachable legacy path removed.** Repository-wide source search found `CopyCredentialToClipboardUseCase` as the only consumer of `CryptoService`; there was no implementation, binding, or caller. The path decrypted to immutable `String`, passed an identifying title as clipboard label, and had no approved owner contract. Removed the unused use case and its unimplemented `CryptoService` interface. Existing credential list/search/save use cases remain. Recompiled and ran the core-domain Android host test task successfully; repository search now finds no remaining symbols or bindings.
- This closes the dead-code disposition for the current exploratory source state. It does not certify any other credential decrypt path or release inventory.

## Clipboard adapter implementation update

- **SEC065-CLIP-ANDROID-001 — Source implementation corrected; host behavior subset passed.** The Android adapter marks clips before publication, schedules one attempt after the fixed 30-second interval, uses only description token metadata for ownership, preserves prior cleanup when a new write is rejected or unconfirmed, and retains bounded receipt outcomes until acknowledgement/abandonment/custody disposition. The host suite now passes 20 synthetic cases covering timing, ownership outcomes, cancellation, stale callback, rejected and silently ignored publication, receipt limits, custody and manual-clear failure. The API 29 Mi A2 has one independently observed foreground attempt and one expected app-switch ownership-unavailable skip. API 28/API 33-35+ physical cohorts, lock/sleep, preview/TalkBack, full race matrix and process-death remain Unverified.
- **SEC065-CLIP-IOS-001 — Source migrated; native verification unavailable on this host.** The iOS adapter now uses `UIPasteboard.changeCount` as metadata, avoids reading clipboard text for ownership, uses bounded receipts and acknowledgement/abandonment, and does not blindly clear after replacement. The requested Gradle task `:core:common:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` was `SKIPPED` on Windows; no iOS compile or runtime result is claimed. Apple documents `changeCount` as a metadata counter incremented when pasteboard contents change ([Apple UIPasteboard.changeCount](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/uipasteboard/changecount)). The check-to-clear race and platform-managed copies remain limitations.
- **SEC065-VAULT-OWNERSHIP-001 — Added active-detail borrower regression.** Six `VaultSubmissionOwnershipTest` cases pass; a submitted operation copy is erased while the selected detail payload remains intact. This is host-level owner-buffer evidence only.

## FIDO ownership host checks

The following selected suites passed on the current exploratory worktree: `CredentialMetadataProtectionServiceOwnershipTest` (11), `GetAssertionCandidateOwnershipTest` (1), `PasskeyEventReplayOwnershipTest` (4), `CredentialRepositoryConsentFlowOwnershipTest` (3), `RelyingPartyRepositoryFlowOwnershipTest` (4), `PasskeyEventStorageOwnershipTest` (15), `AuthenticationConsentStateOwnershipTest` (4), `SensitiveCredentialTextTest` (3), and `RequestEntityOwnershipFailureTest` (2): **47 tests passed, 0 failed**. They include metadata parser/encryption cleanup, malformed/partial decode, cancellation, replay/storage failure, downstream flow failure, owner-copy isolation and request-transfer cleanup.

A fresh current-worktree rerun of the eight T009-selected suites completed 128 tests with zero failures, errors or skips on 2026-11-14; see the appended evidence-index record. T009 is complete for these bounded regression cases. This is selected host-suite evidence, not proof that every source-inventory owner or generated projection is assessed. `SensitiveCredentialText.subSequence` is an internal view that reflects owner erasure; repository search found no production call site for that method beyond its tests, but any later use across suspension must be included in borrower-lifetime assessment. Full flow/replay inventory, SQL projection, CTAP/UI boundaries, heap evidence and device behavior remain open; T009/T012 are not closed by these suites alone.

The structured identifier remains available and must not be logged by callers. | `evidence/optimistic-concurrency-error-message-redaction.md`; red-before synthetic marker test fails on old message, then core domain host suite passes 94 tests/17 reports and KtLint passes. This matrix is intentionally partial. It does not close T006: every inventory owner, generated/database projection, request and flow/replay terminal path, UI-held immutable/provider value, and joined-borrower case must still be mapped or recorded as a bounded finding. No owner may be marked verified solely because a selected host test passed.


| Registration attestation/result (`RegisterCredentialUseCase`, `MakeCredentialResult`) | Result and attestation own credential-ID, AAGUID, RP-hash, authData, signature and challenge arrays independently of the request and stored credential; ownership transfers to the response caller on success. | Staged arrays clear on mapping, signing, persistence or response-construction failure; result cleanup clears owned response arrays and preserves the borrowed request challenge. | Suspend registration failure/cancellation paths clear staged owners; provider signing inputs and returned outputs outside the result lifecycle remain separate. | Credential ID is copied before source credential cleanup; no event fan-out is created by the result object. Caller terminal cleanup and transport/UI borrowers are not exhaustively traced. | `evidence/registration-attestation-independent-credential-id.md`; focused 42-case registration suite and related integration tests pass. Provider/transport/heap ownership remains open (SEC065-CORE-025; T005/T006/T012/T015). |
| CTAPHID channel/session owners (`HidReportParser`, request dispatcher and channel maps) | Channel keys are packed integers; request owns parsed channel/payload arrays until dispatch reaches a terminal outcome. Nested U2F-to-CTAP2 processing receives an independent channel copy. | Parser reset/replacement/expiry and stale, rejected, failed or cancelled request paths erase owned partial/channel/payload arrays; terminal parsed messages are cleared. | Session-generation changes cancel stale work and clear request owners; Bluetooth framework/controller packet copies are beyond this owner. | No immutable channel-ID strings remain in these maps; physical transport delivery and downstream event borrowers are separate owners. | `evidence/ctaphid-channel-owner-cleanup.md`; parser, request owner, dispatcher privacy and session-generation focused tests and full FIDO host suite pass. Physical Bluetooth/OS and heap outcomes remain open (SEC065-CTAP-015; T005/T006/T015/T027). |
| Paired-device ViewModel replay/removal event (`PairedDevicesViewModel`) | StateFlow retains paired-device state while subscribed; pending removal event is owned by ViewModel until consumed or disposed. | Pending removal references clear on ViewModel disposal; replay cache resets after five seconds without subscribers. | `WhileSubscribed` timeout bounds the replay window; Android collector lifecycle and process/session interruption were not verified on device. | Removal events now carry only the MAC address needed by Undo/commit, and the snackbar uses fixed “Device removed” text instead of an alias or Bluetooth name. Host regressions verify the address-only event, replay expiry, and disposal cancellation; the UI lifecycle test source compiles but was not run. Immutable strings, repository/database/native copies, Compose snapshots, concurrent collectors, and heap roots remain open. | `evidence/paired-device-viewmodel-replay-owner-cleanup.md`; two focused cases and full FIDO host suite pass; no device run. (SEC065-FIDO-005; T005/T006/T015/T027). |

Caller must erase each independently owned payload; remaining UI/IME and heap lifetime is open. | `evidence/vault-payload-copy-owner-isolation.md`; both focused regressions failed before the fix and pass afterward; Vault suite passes 93 tests/21 reports with KtLint. (SEC065-VAULT-005; T005/T006/T012/T015). |

The regression uses JDBC in-memory SQLite only; production encrypted-driver/native copies, provider behavior and heap roots remain open under T005/T012/T015. |

The source inventory currently has 696 configured file records and 628 usage records. Its distinct `finding_ids` are not all standalone defects: 230 IDs currently appear in usage rows, including test-fixture and owner-map identifiers. This ledger describes confirmed defects and bounded owner findings; the evidence index and per-usage fields carry the individual test-source dispositions. At this snapshot, 37 distinct IDs from inventory usages were absent from the ledger before this reconciliation; the three substantive owner dispositions above are now recorded. A refreshed inventory comparison classifies the 34 residual references as requirement `NFR-SEC-050` (identified here as a requirement, not a finding) and 33 identifiers attached only to Android host/device/UI tests, instrumentation fixtures or macrobenchmarks. A literal string set difference therefore leaves 33 test-owner labels outside the finding table; these are evidence labels rather than untriaged production defects, and their source usages and scoped results remain in the inventory/evidence rows. The ledger is still incomplete for all current sources, canonical classifications, schema/generated fields and unassessed owners. The original T004 baseline contained 619 files; nine post-baseline privacy test sources were added, and the removed `CryptoService.kt` entry was dropped from the current list. New bounded findings must be added before their source owner is marked assessed; no `Assessed-no-relevant-usage` disposition is inferred from a repository search alone.


## US3 logging flow assessment (partial)

- **SEC065-LOG-008 — BLE session-owner rejection address emission corrected; focused host capture passed.** `BleSessionOwner.connectHost` previously interpolated both attempted and active peer MAC addresses when rejecting a second host. The source now emits a fixed rejection label. The regression confirms both synthetic addresses are absent and the first host remains active after the second connection is rejected. Configured destination capture and broader BLE transport emissions remain open.
- **SEC065-LOG-009 — BLE stale-callback address emissions corrected; focused lifecycle capture passed.** `BleGattTransport` previously logged the peer address for inactive notification callbacks and stale disconnect callbacks. Both now emit fixed labels. A host regression exercised the old peer callbacks after successor connection, confirmed no synthetic address reached Kermit, and confirmed the successor session remained connected. Configured destination capture and other BLE transport emissions remain open.
- **SEC065-LOG-010 — Bluetooth HID transport diagnostic content corrected; focused lifecycle capture passed.** `BluetoothHidTransportImpl` previously logged attempted/connected peer identity, exception messages and state error text, and attached throwables to several errors. These emissions now use fixed labels without throwable forwarding. The new synthetic-address regression failed before correction; the transport lifecycle suite passed afterward. Error-result caller disposition and configured destination capture remain open.
- **SEC065-LOG-011 — Bluetooth HID report sender exception disclosure corrected; focused host capture passed.** `BluetoothHidReportSender.send` previously interpolated platform exception text and forwarded the throwable on send failure. It now emits a fixed failure label; a synthetic exception marker regression failed before correction and then passed while confirming the false result, caller buffer preservation and temporary report erasure. Configured destinations remain unverified.
- **SEC065-LOG-012 — Invalid Bluetooth address throwable forwarding removed; focused wrapper capture passed.** `BluetoothHidDeviceWrapper.connect(macAddress)` previously attached the `IllegalArgumentException` raised by Android when a supplied address is invalid. The wrapper now emits the fixed existing label without the throwable. The synthetic invalid address/exception regression failed before correction and the full wrapper suite passed afterward; the invalid address still fails closed.
- **SEC065-LOG-013 — Vault repository identifier and exception disclosure corrected; focused capture passed.** `VaultRepositoryImpl` previously interpolated item UUIDs in save/delete failures, forwarded caught/result throwables to Kermit, and returned raw exception messages/causes in several `DomainError` values. It now emits fixed failure labels and returns categorized errors with fixed messages and no raw cause. `VaultRepositoryDiagnosticsPrivacyTest` failed before the correction because the synthetic item ID reached captured output; after correction it verified that neither the ID nor synthetic exception marker reached the captured output or returned error. The focused privacy and repository integration suites passed. Configured destination output and callers of other repository error paths still need assessment under T029/T034/T035; this finding does not establish all VaultService error handling as safe.
- **SEC065-LOG-014 — Bluetooth HID proxy object description corrected; focused capture passed.** `BluetoothHidDeviceWrapper.initialize()` previously interpolated the current `BluetoothHidDevice` object in a debug diagnostic before returning from the already-initialized path. The synthetic proxy-description marker reached captured output before correction. The diagnostic now emits only the fixed “initialize called” label; the full wrapper suite passed while retaining the already-initialized fast path. Configured destinations remain unverified under T034.
- **SEC065-ERR-001 — Raw cause conversion has no current production caller.** `Outcome.getOrThrow()` throws an attached `DomainError.cause` unchanged, and `exceptionOrNull()` returns it. The only production `getOrThrow()` consumers found are `SaveCredentialUseCase` and `CreatePasskeyUseCase`; generated Koin factories bind both to `BaseUseCase`, but no production consumer constructs or invokes them. No production `exceptionOrNull()` call was found. T062 records the current source-level disposition; retain the API and reassess if callers are added. This is not runtime diagnostic evidence.
- **SEC065-ERR-002 — Credential-management load failures now use fixed UI text.** `CredentialManagementViewModel` no longer copies `DomainError.message` into its exposed state. The synthetic provider/RP marker regression failed before the correction; all 26 ViewModel tests pass afterward. Other state and presentation error paths remain under review.
- **SEC065-ERR-003 — FIDO error-handler messages now use fixed category text.** `Fido2ErrorHandler` no longer copies domain or throwable messages into `ErrorUi`; the synthetic marker regression failed before correction and all 11 mapped error families pass afterward, preserving titles, retryability and CTAP codes. Other exception-to-UI conversions and rendered-screen behavior remain open.
- **SEC065-ERR-004 — Bluetooth HID connection state no longer exposes caught provider text.** `BluetoothHidTransportImpl.connect` now reports a fixed connection failure to the wrapper state; generated start/disconnect results also use fixed messages. The original typed `Fido2Exception` remains the returned failure for caller classification, and `Fido2ErrorHandler` maps it to safe UI text. The new lifecycle regression failed before correction and all 19 tests pass afterward.
- **SEC065-FIDO-OWNER-002 — Successful credential deletion now ends ViewModel ownership.** `GetAllCredentialsUseCase` returns a paged snapshot, so repository deletion does not update `CredentialManagementViewModel.fullCredentialList` reactively. Both swipe `commitRemove` and biometric-confirmed `deleteCredential` now remove the deleted ID from backing and visible state; the unused `lastDeleted` slot was removed because no caller consumed it. Red regressions for both paths failed before correction on retained state/unerased text, and the full ViewModel suite passes after correction. Renderer lifetime and heap review remain open under T006/T009/T015.
- **SEC065-FIDO-OWNER-003 — Delete-all now releases credential owners and preserves pending snackbar custody.** A red regression demonstrated that repository success left every credential in ViewModel state. Delete-all now removes all backing/visible credentials and transient selection/dialog slots, then erases owners no longer needed. A pending snackbar owner is held in a dedicated map until its terminal Undo/Commit event; the handler recognizes the already committed delete-all and cannot resurrect the row. The focused event-collector barrier verifies readability on both sides of delete-all while the borrower remains active, then erasure after the terminal event; ordinary owners erase immediately.
- **SEC065-LOG-015 — Bluetooth wrapper platform exception forwarding corrected; focused capture passed.** Source search found throwable attachment in unregister, registration and connection permission/state failures. The synthetic `unregisterApp()` failure marker reached Kermit before correction; throwable arguments were removed from all wrapper exception emissions, and the full 31-test wrapper suite passed with state behavior preserved. Configured destinations remain under T034.
- **SEC065-LOG-016 — HMAC extension diagnostics corrected; focused capture passed.** `HmacSecretProcessor` previously included `extensionData::class.simpleName` for unsupported extension values and the supplied salt length for malformed data. The synthetic class-name marker reached captured output before correction. Both diagnostics now use fixed labels; the full four-test processor suite passed with omission and successful processing behavior retained. Configured destinations remain under T034.
- **SEC065-LOG-034 — CTAPHID channel-ID diagnostics corrected; raw host captures passed.** `BluetoothHidTransportImpl`, `CtapHidCommandDispatcher` and `U2fApduCommandHandler` emitted channel IDs in completed-report, CBOR-operation, channel-assignment and U2F diagnostics. All three synthetic registered-channel regressions reached the raw Kermit sink before correction. The emissions now omit channel identifiers while retaining command/payload-length metadata or a fixed channel-assignment label. The 18-case transport lifecycle and 4-case dispatcher/U2F privacy classes pass afterward. Configured Android destinations remain unverified under T029/T034.

The production-source search used `rg -n "Logger\\.|Log\\.(d|i|w|e|v|println)|println\\(|printStackTrace|Timber\\."` while excluding test/build trees. It found emission-related matches in 57 production files across `app/src/main`, `core/common/src`, and FIDO Android/common sources, so the original four `CredentialEncryptionService` edits are not a complete logging audit. A follow-up scoped scan found no direct `NSLog`, `os_log`, `print(`, Kermit, Timber or `Log.` emission in `feature/fido2/src/iosMain` or `core/common/src/iosMain`; that source search does not cover dependencies, generated native code or runtime framework output.

| Emission/destination | Build and registration path | Current finding / evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| FIDO Kermit emissions | `Fido2Initializer.init` builds the writer list and calls `Logger.setLogWriters`; `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter(LogcatWriter())` is added only when `core.common.isDebug`; `LocalCrashReportingLogWriter` is added in all builds and stores severity INFO or higher in `fido2_crash_log.txt`, rotating to `fido2_crash_log.bak`. | Writer-level host tests cover scrubbed messages, fixed non-caller-controlled tags, throwable-message/cause exclusion, final output markers, file failure fallback and rotation. `ChimaliApplication.onCreate` now installs FIDO writers after DI registration but before it launches the asynchronous transport-preference snapshot, closing that explicit startup race. Startup emissions during DI construction and runtime ordering/destination behavior remain unverified; no broader “all FIDO emissions” claim is made. See SEC065-LOG-053. |
| FIDO direct Android logging | `BaselineMeasurements` uses `Log.println(Log.INFO, ...)`; `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink` has a separate Android/Kermit trace path; test stubs and performance harnesses also use direct logging. | `BaselineMeasurements` now accepts a closed `LatencyOperation` enum and its captured output test confirms the fixed operation label; the API 35 direct-Logcat probe passed in isolated diagnostic-debug and minified diagnostic-release targets, with raw/Base64/hex synthetic packet markers absent. The release-derived target uses a diagnostic-only R8 keep rule and is not byte-for-byte product-release evidence. `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink` payload/reachability, other direct sinks and frozen-candidate destinations remain unverified. |
| Remaining interpolated Kermit diagnostics (source triage, 2026-11-16) | Repository-wide `Logger.[vdiwe]` interpolation scan in production `app`, `core`, and `feature` Kotlin sources; reviewed every matched production emission. | Matches interpolate elapsed time, fixed provider-presence booleans, bounded packet/response lengths, fixed CTAP operation labels or protocol opcodes, configured retry/timeout values, negotiated MTU/capacities, and notification/dispatch booleans. No matched line interpolates RP/user/credential values, device aliases/addresses, throwable messages, or caller-provided string identifiers. `BleGattTransport.diagnosticOperationName` maps request bytes to four fixed CTAP operation labels plus `Other`. These remain source-reviewed metadata, not a blanket sink-safety claim; direct Android logging, Koin startup output, destination capture, and non-Logger/framework channels remain separately open under T029/T034/T035. |
| Settings-lookup coroutine trace | `RegisterCredentialUseCase` wraps the settings lookup in `PerformanceTrace.section`; when Android tracing is enabled and an active MakeCredential operation has a trace context, `CoroutineTraceLifecycle` calls `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink`, which emits Kermit INFO events. Payload fields are a random process-session UUID, integer operation/lookup/segment cookies, thread ID, fixed event/outcome labels and booleans. The `Fido2Initializer` installs the INFO+ app-private 5 MiB current/rotated FIDO writer for all builds and the privacy-scrubbed Logcat writer in debug; initialization occurs in `ChimaliApplication.onCreate` after Koin setup but before asynchronous transport snapshot start. | Source review found no RP ID, account, credential, mnemonic, request bytes or caller-provided message in this trace payload. Process-session/cookie values are generated correlation metadata, not user identity. `CoroutineTraceLifecycleTest` passes seven host cases and exercises lifecycle ordering; the Android device probe requires tracing enabled. Neither proves configured destination contents, file retention/deletion, release Logcat behavior or initialization ordering under runtime startup. Map sources to SEC065-LOG-046 and retain those checks under T029/T034/T035. |
| App startup logging | `MainActivity` now emits a direct Android warning only when one or more startup permissions are denied; permission-request branch labels and aggregate-granted messages were removed. | SEC065-LOG-047 removes map interpolation and unnecessary permission-flow disclosures; host tests and API 35 emulator tests against both `shrunkDebug` and non-debuggable minified release confirm a fixed aggregate denial message and no synthetic key in the scoped Logcat slice. Platform retention and other release destinations remain unverified. |
| Other Android Kermit emissions | Source search found device addresses, identifiers, exception messages and dynamic counts/state in Bluetooth, transport, CTAP, crypto, performance and repository code (for example `BluetoothHidCallbackCoordinator`, `BluetoothHidDeviceWrapper`, `WarmUpHelper`, and `VaultRepositoryImpl`). The transport, dispatcher and U2F paths also logged CTAPHID channel IDs. | SEC065-LOG-034 removes all four channel-ID emissions while retaining bounded command/payload-length metadata or a fixed assignment label; SEC065-LOG-035 removes the caller-supplied LatencyProfiler identifier from the missing-start warning; SEC065-LOG-048 removes paired-device enumeration/counts and replaces coordinator bond/connection/adapter/profile/device-class values with fixed event labels; SEC065-LOG-051 removes the MakeCredential `credId=<length>` field and adds captured-output coverage for all terminal modes. Three synthetic registered-CID regressions reached captured output before correction, and all 22 cases across the transport lifecycle and dispatcher/U2F privacy classes pass afterward. Remaining dynamic emissions require exact caller reachability and final-destination review. Scrubber heuristics are supplementary and do not establish safety; each reachable dynamic emission must be dispositioned or fixed with an output regression. |
| Vault repository Kermit errors | `VaultRepositoryImpl` is bound as `VaultService` in production DI and used by the vault presentation layer. It emitted repository failures through global Kermit, including UUID-bearing save/delete messages and throwable content; it also returned dynamic exception text and causes through `Outcome.Error`. | SEC065-LOG-013 removes those source values from diagnostics and returned errors. The synthetic failure is reachable through the repository regression, but the configured production writer registration/order and final Logcat/file destinations remain unverified under T029/T034. |
| iOS native logging | The scoped `feature/fido2` and `core/common` iOS-source search found no direct `NSLog`, `os_log`, or `print` calls. | Search result is not proof of no destination; linked frameworks, generated/native code, and runtime output have not been enumerated or observed. |
| Writer throwable and fallback output | `LocalCrashReportingLogWriter` and `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter` could retain caller-controlled `StackTraceElement` fields even after removing throwable messages and causes. | Synthetic frame markers failed in both local output and delegated Kermit output before correction. The local writer now emits only `[throwable details omitted]`; the debug wrapper forwards a fixed-message throwable with no stack frames. The 15-case writer suite passes with severity and rotation behavior retained. Fallback diagnostics remain fixed; the host regression forces a path failure containing a synthetic marker and confirms neither path nor exception text reaches stderr. | Android Logcat/file runtime destinations and initialized production ordering remain unverified. |

### T029 emission-to-destination matrix (source review; runtime destinations unverified)

| Emission path / build reachability | Transform and destination | Source-level result and remaining evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Kermit `Logger` calls in production modules | `Fido2Initializer.init` installs `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter(LogcatWriter())` only when `core.common.isDebug`; installs `LocalCrashReportingLogWriter` for both builds. The privacy wrapper scrubs message text, replaces the tag with `Fido2`, and forwards a fixed throwable with an empty stack. The local writer ignores caller tags, scrubs the message, replaces throwable details with a fixed line, and writes INFO+ to `filesDir/logs/fido2_crash_log.txt`; rotation uses `fido2_crash_log.bak`. | Source inspection establishes configured writers and filtering, not which entries Android actually receives or whether a release build excludes/retains each call site. Host tests cover transformation, rotation, fallback, and synthetic markers; Android Logcat and internal-file captures for debug and release remain unverified. |
| Startup loggers and FIDO writer registration | `ChimaliApplication.onCreate` starts Koin with its default `EmptyLogger`, then calls `Fido2Initializer.init` to register the Kermit writers, then starts `ProcessTransportSelection.startSnapshot()` asynchronously. The explicit Koin `androidLogger()` override was removed; Koin 4.2.1 initializes its default logger as `EmptyLogger`. `Fido2Initializer` itself emits its initialization message only after `Logger.setLogWriters`. | Source order closes the identified transport-snapshot-before-registration race and removes Koin's separate Android logging destination during DI construction. The updated isolated diagnosticDebug package launched on API 35; a PID-filtered query for `KOIN`/`Koin` tags returned zero records, and the package was removed. This bounded capture covers those tags and that startup run, not all Android output or startup failures. Other dependency/provider output, pre-registration Kermit emissions and release/candidate behavior remain open under T034/T035. |
| Writer fallback and local storage | Writer initialization, append/write, and rotation failures emit fixed `System.err` labels; local files are in app-private `filesDir/logs/`, with current and single backup files. The writer records INFO+ only, so debug-only entries do not enter the file. | Host failure injection verifies marker/path/exception omission on stderr and host rotation behavior. Device file permissions, actual Android Logcat routing, file contents across app lifecycle, and fallback behavior on-device remain unverified. |
| Direct Android logging | `MainActivity` emits permission-flow messages using `android.util.Log`; `BaselineMeasurements` emits timing summaries using `Log.println`; `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink` uses its own Android/Kermit trace route. These bypass or are independent of the `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter` → `LogcatWriter` chain. | `BaselineMeasurements` accepts only the fixed `LatencyOperation` enum for operation identifiers. Its API 35 direct-Logcat probe passed in diagnostic-debug and minified diagnostic-release-derived targets: expected fixed labels/timing fields appeared and synthetic response bytes were absent in raw, Base64 and hexadecimal forms. The release-derived diagnostic target uses an isolated R8 keep rule and is not product-signed or byte-for-byte product release evidence. `MainActivity` permission values, `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink`, full reachability, other sinks and frozen-candidate output remain separately unverified. Performance measurement output remains available for its evidence protocol. |
| Remaining FIDO protocol, repository and presentation emissions | Source review covered `BluetoothAdapterStateWaiter`, `Ctap2ResetAuthenticatorHandler`, `Ctap2ResponseBuilder`, `PostQuantumCrypto`, `Fido2CryptoService`, `CredentialRepositoryImpl`, `CredentialRepositoryReadOperations`, `CtapHidCommandDispatcher`, `U2fApduCommandHandler`, `SelectCredentialUseCase`, `CredentialListScreen`, `Fido2UiEventBus`, `AuthenticationPromptViewModel`, and `RegistrationPromptViewModel`. | These calls emit fixed operation/state labels, closed crypto-operation constants, event/state class names, fixed RP/user-independent error text, or bounded protocol metadata such as APDU instruction/length, CTAPHID command, packet count, credential count choice, and AAGUID length. Repository read-helper messages are compile-time literals at every callsite. No RP/user text, credential IDs, payload bytes or provider exception messages are interpolated in the reviewed calls. | Source disposition only. These paths route through configured Kermit unless a build removes them; no per-emission Android destination capture or release reachability proof was performed for this grouped set. Recheck dynamic callsite inputs and configured sinks under T029/T034/T035.
| Performance, trace and logger-support emissions | Source review covered `AndroidLookupTraceEventSink`, `PerformanceTrace` and its constant callsites, `LatencyProfiler`, `WarmUpHelper`, `BaselineMeasurements`, `BouncyCastleLoader`, `Fido2Initializer`, `LocalCrashReportingLogWriter`, `PrivacyLogScrubber`, and `TraceEntry.toString`. | Trace events use closed outcome/action enums, integer correlation/thread/segment fields and booleans; trace section names are constants at current callers. Latency/warm-up output uses fixed operation labels and elapsed timing; Bouncy Castle startup includes only a presence boolean. `TraceEntry.toString` redacts caller descriptions and metadata. Direct `BaselineMeasurements` output has separate API 35 debug and minified release-derived process captures (SEC065-CORE-021). | Source mapping and bounded captures only: Android trace visibility, other logger destinations, system/consumer retention, release reachability and full candidate output remain unverified. The test-only release-derived target is not product-signed/frozen-candidate evidence; keep T029/T034/T035 open.
| iOS native source logging | Current-tree enumeration found 9 Kotlin/Swift/Objective-C source files under all `src/iosMain` roots. A search across those files for direct `NSLog`, `os_log`, `print`, Kermit `Logger`/`Kermit`, Timber, and Android `Log` calls found no matches. The reviewed `IosClipboardManagerService` also contains no logger calls and returns fixed generic publication/clear errors without forwarding caught exception content. | This is source-search evidence only; dependencies, generated native code, framework output, compilation, and iOS runtime destinations have not been enumerated or captured. |

Per-file destination classification of the census hits (2026-11-19 source pass): each line listed in `evidence/diagnostic-source-census.md` was re-read and classified by token kind. **53 files / ~360 hits are Kermit `Logger` calls**, which reach only the writers installed by `Fido2Initializer.init` (debug: `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter(LogcatWriter)`; configured: `LocalCrashReportingLogWriter` app-private files with rotation and fixed `System.err` fallback; writer registration now precedes async transport startup per SEC065-LOG-053). **`MainActivity.kt`** has 1 `android.util.Log` call ? direct Logcat. **`LocalCrashReportingLogWriter.kt`** has 4 `println`/`System.err` emissions ? the writer's own fixed fallback/stderr path. **`BaselineMeasurements.kt`** has 2 `Log.println` timing emissions ? direct Logcat (fixed `LatencyOperation` labels per SEC065-CORE-021). No census file emits via `NSLog`, `os_log`, or Timber; the iOS matrix row stands. This classification is syntactic reachability to a destination class only — per-emission content review remains with the linked findings and T034/T035 runtime verification.
Message, tag, throwable, cause, and object-description handling is assessed per source finding above; writer-level marker tests do not establish safety for a source emission that bypasses those writers. T029 remains open until the inventory-discovered production emissions are mapped to reachable build paths and the configured Android destinations (including pre-registration, current/rotated files, and fallback) are captured. iOS runtime destination status remains Unverified.

Dynamic-interpolation refresh (2026-11-20 source pass): a fresh scan of production Kotlin/Java sources (`app/src/main`, `app/src/clipboardAudit`, `core`, `feature`; test/androidTest/heapAudit/build trees excluded) re-enumerated logger call sites and extracted every interpolated string literal in their message arguments — 96 dynamic candidates, all bounded metadata consistent with the 2026-11-16 review: payload/report/packet lengths, attempt/retry counters, elapsed milliseconds, negotiated MTU/capacity/timeout values, CTAPHID/CTAP/U2F/COSE protocol integers rendered as hex or decimal, booleans, fixed operation labels, `aaguid`/output sizes, QR `tokenCount`, provider-presence booleans, and Kotlin `::class.simpleName` values. The simple-name emissions are a closed set in practice: `Ctap2GetAssertionHandler` emits `e::class.simpleName` only for `IllegalArgumentException` from the `PrfExtensionInput` constructor, and the prompt ViewModels emit their own state-class names; neither carries caller or exception-message content. No RP/user/credential text, device aliases/addresses, throwable messages or caller-provided identifiers appear in the scanned set. This is a syntactic re-verification on the dirty worktree, not a semantic re-proof of every callsite's runtime reachability; per-emission caller-input review, configured-destination captures and frozen-candidate behavior remain open under T029/T034/T035.

`LocalCrashReportingLogWriterTest` passed 15 cases on 2026-11-13 exploratory revision after throwable-field suppression. The Bluetooth callback/wrapper and provider warm-up source-emission suites passed 68 cases. Before the 2026-11-14 legacy-service removal, the credential encryption and KeyStore privacy suites passed 15 selected cases; these historical tests are not current-tree evidence. `RawCtapOperationTest` plus `CtapHidCommandDispatcherPrivacyTest` passed 9 cases, and `PairedDeviceRepositoryPrivacyTest` passed 1 case. These host tests do not establish Kermit initialization order, Android OS Logcat captures, release build reachability, or iOS runtime output. T029, T033–T035 remain open until the remaining source rows and destination evidence are complete; T032 writer remediation is verified on the current tree.

## Per-owner outcome summary (partial)

| Owner / usage | Success | Failure / cancellation | Partial or never-started transfer | Flow/replay and borrower disposition | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Android password clipboard service | Mutable input is borrowed and platform string conversion is bounded to the API boundary; accepted receipt carries only generation/deadline/publication status. | Publication/manual-clear cancellation propagates; publication rejection preserves prior receipt work; automatic metadata/error outcomes are bounded and sanitized. | No receipt is created on admission rejection; uncertainty blocks a subsequent publish; pending work can be transferred to the application custodian. | Two-slot state retains terminal statuses until acknowledgement/abandonment; synchronization coverage exists for representative terminal/handoff cases but the entire race matrix is not closed. | Partial; physical/device tasks open. |
| Android application backup/transfer policy (`AndroidManifest.xml`) | The package manifest disables Android backup and references backup/data-extraction policy resources; it does not acquire or transform a secret. | Platform/OEM backup and restore behavior is not exercised here; any policy mismatch or ignored restriction remains unknown. | No application secret buffer is created by this owner; the policy is packaged with the app and applied by the platform during transfer/restore. | No in-process borrower; Android backup/transfer services may operate on persisted app data and must honor the packaged policy. | Source-only mapping; runtime/OEM behavior remains Unverified. |
| Application severe-memory-trim callback (`ChimaliApplication.onTrimMemory`) | Severe trim is delegated to `WalletSessionMemoryPressure`, which invokes `MasterSeedProvider.clearSession`; host trim-policy tests cover the policy helper. | Exceptions or callback timing in the actual Application lifecycle are not exercised on device; provider/JCA object erasure remains unverified. | The callback holds no seed copy and does not initialize the provider before trim; provider-owned session arrays remain owned by the provider. | Provider session coordination joins active owner release; `onTrimMemory` is a process lifecycle trigger, not proof every borrower has terminated. | Source wiring and host policy only; Android callback and heap behavior remain open. |
| Foreground seed owner lifecycle (`MainActivity.onStop`) | `onStop` requests cancellation of foreground seed reveal/import owners; active authenticator ceremonies use a separate owner kind. | Provider cancellation and borrower-join behavior are covered by host owner tests; lifecycle dispatch and Compose/renderer release are not device-verified. | An operation not yet admitted owns no provider borrow; once admitted, cancellation is delegated to the provider's session-owner mechanism. | `WalletMasterSeedProvider` waits for admitted borrowers before erasing shared session data; full UI collector/replay and heap-root coverage remain open. | Source mapping and host borrower regressions; device and heap evidence remain open. |

Cancellation follows Compose collection lifetime; serializer/DataStore internal buffers are not proven erased. | The complete snapshot is borrowed for projection; no seed field is copied into `AppRoutingState`, but the upstream immutable ciphertext remains under DataStore/Flow/GC ownership. | Each emission is mapped to a route projection; subscriber replay/latest-value and complete-snapshot lifetime are not measured. | Source mapping only; immutable snapshot and serializer/provider behavior remain open. |

Provider/native copies and heap roots are not measured. | Corrected and host-verified at application-array boundary; provider and heap evidence remain open under T006/T015. |

Driver/native row buffers and Android runtime copies are not observed. | Source and integration host evidence only; provider and heap behavior remain open under T006/T015. |

Runtime roots/accessibility copies are not measured. | Unresolved framework text boundary; T006/T015/T027 remain open. |

The governing rule remains: “An owner cannot erase while an admitted borrower remains active.” This matrix records known cases and open transitions; it is not an attestation that every owner, failure route, flow, or runtime root has been reviewed.

### SEC065-DB-010 — partial event-store batch decode

`EventStoreRepositoryImpl.getEvents` and `getEventsFrom` previously decoded rows with `map`. If a later row failed, the method never transferred the already-decoded prefix, but those `VaultEvent` title and payload owners were not explicitly erased. Both paths now use `decodeOwnedVaultEvents`, which transfers only a fully decoded batch and clears the partial list in `finally` otherwise. The new synthetic regression fails when the cleanup action is removed and passes with it. The focused `EventStoreRepositoryImplTest` suite also passes (5 cases). The finding is corrected at the application-owned mutable-array boundary; T005/T012/T015 remain open for complete owner mapping, provider/string copies and runtime heap evidence.

### SEC065-VAULT-015 — Vault event escaped the staging list on admission failure

`decodeOwnedVaultEvents` already erased all events present in its local batch if a later row failed, but it appended each decode result inline. If `MutableList.add` failed after `decode(row)` returned, that current `VaultEvent` was not in the cleanup list and its title/payload arrays remained populated. The helper now stages the decoded event in a local variable and clears it if list admission fails; earlier admitted events remain covered by the outer batch `finally`. This is a source correction only; no regression test was added or run. The existing partial-decode test does not exercise list-admission failure. Successful caller release, provider/string copies and heap evidence remain open under T006/T012/T015.


### SEC065-VAULT-002 — title-bearing Vault JSON materialization

Production event and snapshot repositories previously used the generic kotlinx.serialization JSON API on Vault models. `SensitiveCharArraySerializer` converted title arrays to immutable `String` values during encoding, and decrypted bytes were converted to immutable JSON `String` values during decoding. This path was active despite the serializer comment claiming these repositories already used a mutable-field codec.

Added `VaultMutableJsonCodec` and routed Vault event append/read and snapshot save/read through it. The codec writes the legacy v1 property layout through `ErasableCharBuffer`, encodes directly to a mutable UTF-8 byte array, decodes UTF-8 into a mutable character array, and copies title data into caller-owned `CharArray` values. A current source search found `VaultEventSerializer` was only used in tests; its immutable JSON path and duplicate `EventStoreSerializerTest` have now been removed. Frozen compatibility fixtures are checked through the active mutable codec, and repository cleanup tests no longer create immutable plaintext JSON strings.

The current full `:core:data:testDebugUnitTest` suite passes 43 tests across 9 reports after the legacy serializer/test removal, and `:core:data:ktlintCheck` passes. The parser erases partially built JSON trees, input character storage and any title/payload arrays not transferred to a returned model; malformed-content buffer cleanup regressions pass. The finding remains Corrected-unverified because T005/T006/T012/T015 still require full owner reconciliation, provider/native ownership and heap evidence.

## Managed, provider, platform-text and heap limits

These limits describe what the current implementation and evidence can establish. They are not waivers for application-owned buffers, and they do not close release acceptance.

| Boundary | Current evidence and limit | Required handling |
|---|---|---|
| JVM `BigInteger` intermediates | `P256ParentKeyDerivation` and `HdkP256` use `BigInteger` for private scalar arithmetic. The Java API defines `BigInteger` as immutable, so these values cannot be overwritten through their API; arithmetic may also allocate intermediates. The repository explicitly keeps these values operation-local and erases the mutable byte-array inputs/outputs, but this is not erasure of the integer objects. ([Java `BigInteger`](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/24/docs/api/java.base/java/math/BigInteger.html)) | Do not convert the limitation into a blanket no-erasure claim. Keep operation scope narrow, erase application-owned byte arrays promptly, and treat retained or provider-created objects as unresolved until heap evidence can attribute them. |
| Bouncy Castle P-256 parameters | The app pins Bouncy Castle 1.80. Its `ECPrivateKeyParameters` stores a `BigInteger` scalar and exposes `getD()`; the exact dependency JAR's API inspection shows no `destroy()` method on that class or its `AsymmetricKeyParameter` base. This is a limitation of the lightweight parameter class used by the library, not a statement about every Bouncy Castle private-key type. ([Bouncy Castle 1.80 `ECPrivateKeyParameters`](https://javadoc.io/static/org.bouncycastle/bcprov-jdk18on/1.80/org/bouncycastle/crypto/params/ECPrivateKeyParameters.html), [base class](https://javadoc.io/static/org.bouncycastle/bcprov-jdk18on/1.80/org/bouncycastle/crypto/params/AsymmetricKeyParameter.html)) | Do not promise key-object zeroization. Bound its lifetime to the operation, erase mutable serialized scalar buffers, and record the object/provider lifetime as unresolved pending actual runtime evidence. |
| Android ICU normalization | `Bip39MasterSeedGenerator` writes NFKD output through the ICU `Normalizer2.normalize(CharSequence, Appendable)` overload into an app-owned `ErasableCharBuffer`; it avoids explicitly receiving ICU's `String`-returning overload result. Android documents that normalization produces normalized text, but does not promise erasure of internal native/managed temporary storage. ([Android `Normalizer2`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/icu/text/Normalizer2)) | The mutable destination is erased on every exit. ICU internal allocation and cleanup are not measured and must remain an explicit provider limitation, not treated as verified absent. |
| Android/iOS platform text | Android's secure clipboard guidance requires sensitive clip marking before publication to obscure previews; Chimali applies `ClipDescription.EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE`. It does not control clipboard history, IMEs, system/provider copies, process-stop scheduling, or whether the platform accepts/clears a clip. The selected 30-second cleanup remains best effort and ownership-checked. iOS uses change-count metadata and is not certified by Android results. ([Android secure clipboard handling](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/risks/secure-clipboard-handling), [Android `ClipDescription.EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/ClipDescription#EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE)) | Do not claim platform-wide erasure or a hard deadline. Keep observable app-owner handling separate from OS/provider behavior; physical cohorts and iOS verification remain open. |
| Heap and native/provider visibility | Android Java heap captures are snapshots; the platform distinguishes Java heap data from native allocations, and Perfetto Java heap capture omits field contents. Heap tooling itself may affect collection. Five controlled SEC065 HPROF runs now inspect synthetic marker fields, arrays and Java GC-root paths across the required groups; see `evidence/heap-inspection-completion-api35-20261117.md`. ([Android Java memory analysis](https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/memory/guide/java-memory), [Android heap capture](https://developer.android.com/studio/profile/capture-heap-dump)) | T015's defined synthetic procedure is complete. No-match after collection, host tests, Perfetto reference-only graphs or LeakCanary alone prove neither content absence nor provider/native erasure. Release-equivalent and broader platform/native boundaries remain Unverified. |

The exact current build still has unresolved application-owned immutable strings and unreviewed diagnostic/storage routes, so these documented constraints do not establish compliance.

### SEC065-CTAP-010 — Partial mutable text conversion failure

`SensitiveCredentialText.copyOf` previously filled a newly allocated `CharArray` directly from a borrowed `CharSequence`. If a source accessor threw after one or more characters, the partial destination became unreachable without being erased. `copyCharacters` now wipes the destination on any thrown failure, and `copyOf` retains responsibility until construction completes. The synthetic mid-copy regression passed in `:feature:fido2:testAndroidHostTest`. A follow-up test found length validation outside the cleanup guard: a changing `CharSequence.length` could leave a pre-populated destination uncleared. Validation now runs inside the guarded copy block; this regression failed before correction, then all five focused `SensitiveCredentialTextTest` cases and FIDO KtLint passed. Broader conversion, immutable-boundary and heap-lifecycle review remains open under T012/T015.

### SEC065-CTAP-013 — Base64 challenge factory partial construction

`MakeCredentialOptions.fromBase64Challenge` decoded a challenge to a mutable byte array, then called `create` without cleanup if validation of another option failed. The factory now keeps the decoded buffer locally until the options object is successfully constructed, erasing it on every failed exit and transferring it on success. `MakeCredentialOptionsTest` directly observes both failed-construction erasure and successful transfer. This closes the bounded factory path; immutable compatibility projections and the wider T012/T015 conversion and heap review remain open.

### SEC065-LOG-039 — Paired-device repository diagnostic privacy

`PairedDeviceRepositoryPrivacyTest` injects a synthetic Bluetooth address and database exception through alias-update and delete failures, confirming the typed error is retained without exposing either marker in captured diagnostics or retaining the raw cause. The current source and regression are now represented as separate relevant inventory usages. Focused host evidence is linked in the evidence index; save-path and configured Android destination coverage remain open.

A current-filesystem comparison against the 14 modules included by `settings.gradle.kts` reconciles all 655 files under configured `src/**` roots; none are missing or stale, and all recorded source hashes match. The restored additions include 17 Relevant privacy, clipboard-observer and configured-diagnostic source/test records and 24 inspected no-relevant-use placeholder/resource records. The 42 usage rows have valid bidirectional file links and canonical IDs, but 590 file dispositions remain Open and the complete prior owner ledger is not restored. T005 remains open for owner enumeration and generated-schema/runtime reconciliation.

The inventory usage ledger was extended with 29 structured owner records recovered from prior reviewed schema and persistence edits: optional consent model/DAO/use case/query; Vault and FIDO schemas/migrations; shared preference model/serializer/DataStore and platform bindings; encrypted driver production wiring/tests; and encrypted-metadata DAO cleanup. They retain current source paths and are linked bidirectionally. Provider/native copies, generated signatures, retention, and frozen-candidate evidence remain open.

### SEC065-CLIP-IOS-002 — Scheduled cleanup interruption bookkeeping

Source review found that cancellation during the iOS adapter's delay could leave a pending receipt and retained expected-change-count record without a terminal outcome. Intentional supersede/manual-clear cancellation also cancelled the job before removing its guard, allowing the cancellation callback to race with the intended terminal outcome. The adapter now records `Interrupted` for a still-current scheduled job, clears its one-shot metadata under the serialized owner, and removes the ownership guard before intentional cancellation. The source hash was refreshed in `source-inventory.json`. Kotlin/Native compilation is disabled on this Windows host and no iOS test target/runtime was available, so this correction remains Unverified pending a macOS compile and iOS behavior check; the change does not establish UIKit pasteboard removal.


### Current app lifecycle owner review (2026-11-14)

Mapped the application manifest's backup/transfer policy, ChimaliApplication.onTrimMemory, and MainActivity.onStop to their secret-owner responsibilities. The manifest disables backup and references `app/src/main/res/xml/backup_rules.xml` and `app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml`; both resources explicitly exclude local storage domains and now have separate `CLS-KEY-001` inventory usages linked to SEC065-DB-006. Process trim delegates to MasterSeedProvider.clearSession; the foreground activity cancels foreground owner sessions. Android/OEM backup enforcement and restore/transfer behavior, actual lifecycle dispatch, callback instrumentation and post-terminal heap evidence remain unresolved (T005/T006/T015).


### App navigation and retained-screen owners (2026-11-14)

Source review confirms the navigation DataStore flow receives full UserPreferences snapshots, including the immutable encryptedWalletSeed field, then projects only route and feature flags. AppShellWithOverlay intentionally keeps the main feature subtree mounted under Settings; the Compose test asserts the shell and pending ceremony remain alive. This is not treated as a terminal interaction: Vault/FIDO screen owners remain responsible for cleanup at their own terminal events. The upstream immutable preference snapshot and retained UI/ViewModel heap lifetime are still unverified and are now represented in the inventory and partial owner matrix.

### SEC065-CLIP-IOS-003 — Schedule from publication time

The iOS adapter returned a receipt deadline calculated from the successful pasteboard write but started a fresh full delay after coroutine dispatch, which could extend the attempt beyond that recorded deadline. It now samples monotonic uptime immediately after publication and delays only for the remaining time to `copiedAt + 30_000`; if dispatch is already late, the one-shot attempt becomes eligible immediately. This aligns the source schedule with the accepted best-effort contract but does not guarantee callback execution or pasteboard removal. KtLint passes; Kotlin/Native compilation is skipped on this Windows host, so the behavior remains Unverified pending macOS compilation and iOS runtime evidence.

### SEC065-CLIP-IOS-004 — iOS clipboard adapter diagnostic source review

Reviewed every branch in `IosClipboardManagerService` and its `ClipboardError` construction. The adapter has no logger, print, `NSLog`, or platform diagnostic sink. Publication/clear failures discard caught exception text and return fixed error messages; cleanup status contains only generation, fixed outcome, and origin. This resolves the requested source-level diagnostic review without a code change because no emission was present. It does not establish Kotlin/Native compilation, native exception behavior, or configured iOS logging outside this adapter; those remain Unverified.
### SEC065-CTAP-011 — immutable legacy Base64 clientDataHash conversion

The compatibility branch in `Ctap2GetAssertionHandler.decodeOptions` called `SensitiveCredentialText.toBoundaryString()` before Base64 decoding, creating a new immutable encoded challenge String. The mutable source owner was request-scoped, but this additional plaintext representation had no erasure path.

The handler now reads the borrowed `CharSequence` directly into temporary ASCII bytes, rejects non-ASCII input, decodes with `Base64.Decoder.decode(ByteArray)`, and clears the staging array in `finally`. The existing separately tracked decoded hash remains erased at validation failure, cancellation and request completion. A new test observed the staging array as nonzero before cleanup and failed because no mutable staging owner existed; after correction, success, later-validation failure and cancellation all verify both encoded staging and decoded hash arrays are zero. All 18 `Ctap2GetAssertionHandlerTest` cases pass and `:feature:fido2:ktlintCheck` passes. Provider-internal copies and post-terminal heap roots remain open under T012/T015.

### SEC065-CTAP-012 — CTAP registration option borrower lifetime

**Status: Source remediation and focused host integration complete; platform and heap review remain open.** `Ctap2MakeCredentialHandler.handle` creates an independent options copy for the UI, so request cleanup can erase decoded request owners without touching the UI borrower. `Fido2UiEventBus` gives one presenter a synchronized claim; if no presenter claims an event, deferred completion erases its options. `RegistrationPromptViewModel` defers option erasure until its request parent and child operations complete. This addresses the source-level shared-buffer cancellation race.

The original trace identified a plausible risk; it did not reproduce data corruption. Eight `MakeCredentialOptionsTest`, six `Fido2UiEventBusTest`, 24 `Ctap2MakeCredentialHandlerValidationTest`, and 25 `RegistrationOwnerIntegrationTest` cases pass with FIDO KtLint. These prove independent source/copy erasure, unclaimed and never-started event cleanup, claimed retention, keepalive and dispatch-admission failure cleanup, and deferred erasure while canceled child borrowers are held during replacement and ViewModel disposal. T091 is complete. Android runtime/provider-copy and heap-root lifetimes remain outside this source/host evidence; T005/T006/T012 remain open for broader owner reconciliation. Exact owners are listed as `U-FIDO-USER-ENTITY-OWNER-001`, `U-FIDO-MAKE-CREDENTIAL-OPTIONS-OWNER-001`, `U-REG-PROMPT-OPTIONS-OWNER-001`, and event bus/host-test usages.

### SEC065-KEY-003 — Unsupported credential algorithm exception detail

`CredentialKeyDescriptor.profileFor` included the caller-supplied unsupported COSE algorithm integer in its exception message. The new synthetic assertion failed before correction. The exception now uses fixed wording, and the three-case descriptor class passes. The six-case FIDO algorithm-selection class also passes, preserving supported algorithm behavior. The owner map now traces credential ID, `cose_algorithm`, and `derivation_profile` from the serializable descriptor through the passkey DAO bind and SQLDelight schema/query projections; the key ID remains immutable TEXT, algorithm metadata is INTEGER, and profile metadata is TEXT. Generated row/driver behavior, identifier association/retention, and deletion/heap evidence remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015.

### SEC065-CRYPTO-005 — P-256 HDK/HKDF mutable and immutable intermediate owners

The source review and tests cover three distinct owner paths. `HdkP256` erases application-owned digest/preimage/context and untransferred arrays; its successful factor and child scalar transfer to a result for caller cleanup after signing. `HkdfSha256` clears temporary mutable intermediates and returns separately-owned outputs, while JCA/`SecretKeySpec` copies remain unobservable. `P256Group` clears temporary serialized scalar bytes but uses immutable `BigInteger` and `ECPoint` objects for secret arithmetic; those remain operation-local rather than explicitly erasable. The complete core-security host suite passes 72 tests in 13 reports, including 6 HDK, 9 HKDF and 9 group cases. These are host contracts only; provider/runtime copies and heap roots remain open under T015.

### SEC065-UV-002 — Orphaned platform user-verification abstraction

`PlatformUserVerification` and its Android and iOS implementations had no production references, bindings, or callers; the FIDO feature uses a separate `UserVerificationServiceImpl`. The duplicate interface, both platform implementations, and the direct Android adapter test were removed. A host class-loading regression failed before removal and passes afterward. The complete current core-security host suite passes 69 tests across 13 reports, and KtLint passes. iOS compilation/runtime is not verified on this Windows host; the active FIDO capability service's runtime/cache behavior and heap lifetime remain open (T005/T006/T015/T027).

### SEC065-UV-003 — Wall-clock rollback extended availability cache

`UserVerificationServiceImpl` used wall-clock epoch milliseconds for the 10-second cached availability TTL. If device time moved backward, a negative cache age satisfied the freshness check and could extend cached device-posture metadata until wall time caught up. The TTL now uses Android monotonic elapsed realtime; wall clock remains only for the enrollment timestamp. A deterministic host regression with backward wall-clock samples verifies cache reuse before the deadline and refresh exactly at expiry. The test passes. No production invalidation caller was found. This fixes TTL correctness but does not prove Android platform behavior, caller/UI release or heap cleanup (T005/T006/T015/T027).

Source review mapped three app instrumentation tests previously left Open. `CredentialCryptoProviderTest` generates ephemeral synthetic P-256, Ed25519 and ML-DSA private-key objects and checks target-APK provider resolution, public-key decoding and signatures; provider/JCA/native copies are opaque. `LeakCanaryMnemonicOwnerTest` imports a known synthetic phrase into a UUID-scoped encrypted test DataStore, asserts import and revealed `CharArray` cleanup, closes the activity, clears the provider session and deletes the fixture directory; LeakCanary's lifecycle assertion is not heap-field/root evidence. `ProductionAppProcessRestartTest` stores the PID and synthetic credential ID between two instrumented production-app invocations, verifies a new process recovers/signs with the persisted credential, clears mutable challenge/message/signature arrays and provider session, and removes the fixture directory; the dedicated runner force-stops and uninstalls both disposable APKs in `finally`. A runner interrupted before that cleanup is not covered. The three sources are Relevant under the applicable key/FIDO classifications; no connected instrumentation or HPROF review was performed. See `evidence/android-app-security-test-owners.md`. T005/T006/T015 remain open.

### SEC065-CORE-024 — Sanitized passkey JSON copy on capacity failure

`SensitiveJsonFields.encode` created the sanitized serialization byte array, then calculated and allocated the replacement buffer before entering the `try/finally` that erased the sanitized copy. Integer capacity overflow or buffer allocation failure could therefore bypass the sanitized-array cleanup. Capacity arithmetic is now checked and performed inside the cleanup scope; the owned output buffer is also erased when initialized. A deterministic regression supplies a repeating-value map that overflows capacity arithmetic without a large allocation, retains the sanitized array and asserts it is zeroed after the exception. The prior test-first build failed because the cleanup helper did not exist; the focused `PasskeyEventStorageOwnershipTest` passes after correction. Successful return transfers the encoded bytes to the repository caller, which clears them after encryption. SQL/provider copies and replay/aggregate borrower completion remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015.

The same bridge's parser previously copied every parsed object property name into an immutable `String` and retained growing path lists solely to select the configured extraction path. It now keeps only an integer matched-path depth and compares the mutable parsed key characters directly to static path components before erasing them. One-level and nested exact-path regressions pass in the 19-case `PasskeyEventStorageOwnershipTest`; the full FIDO host suite passes 1,318 tests across 172 reports with zero failures/errors/skips, and FIDO KtLint passes. This is a source-level projection reduction, not heap proof; SQL/provider copies and replay/aggregate borrower completion remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015. See `evidence/passkey-event-json-path-projection-cleanup.md`.


### SEC065-CRYPTO-008 — Android Keystore datastore AES-GCM owner cleanup

`EncryptionWrapper.encrypt` previously cleared caller plaintext only after successful cipher initialization and `doFinal`, and left its copied IV and intermediate ciphertext arrays populated. Failures during cipher creation or Keystore key lookup occurred before the cleanup path. `decrypt` copied the IV and encrypted payload but did not clear either on authentication failure or success. The implementation now consumes plaintext in an outer `finally` spanning cipher/key setup, clears application-owned IV/ciphertext temporaries on all exits, and clears decrypt slices in `finally` while leaving the caller's envelope borrowed and unchanged. Four Android-host regressions cover successful independent output, invalid-key setup failure, cipher/key lookup failures, and decrypt success/authentication failure. The full Android Keystore instrumentation sources now clear their synthetic fixtures, but were not run in this session. JCA/GCMParameterSpec/Android Keystore provider copies and heap roots remain unresolved under T005/T006/T015.


### SEC065-FIDO-005 — Paired-device ViewModel cache and buffered event retention

`PairedDevicesViewModel.pairedDevices` had an infinite replay expiration, allowing its last list of paired Bluetooth addresses, aliases, and device metadata to remain referenced for the ViewModel lifetime after UI collection stopped. Its removal channel also buffered a full `PairedDevice` even though Undo/commit need only the MAC address, and the snackbar interpolated the alias or Bluetooth name. The sharing policy now resets replay after the existing five-second stop timeout; `onCleared` empties pending deletion state and cancels the removal channel. The channel now carries only the address, and the snackbar uses fixed “Device removed” text. Host regressions cover replay expiry, disposal cancellation, and the address-only event; the UI lifecycle test source verifies that the alias is absent from snackbar semantics and compiles, but was not run on a device. The complete FIDO host suite passes 1,327 tests across 172 XML reports with zero failures, errors, or skips; module KtLint passes. Database/native copies, immutable strings, framework snapshots, runtime lifecycle and heap-root behavior remain open.

This bounds app-owned references only. `PairedDevice` uses immutable Strings that cannot be explicitly erased; repository/database/native copies, actual Compose collector disposal, backup/deleted-page behavior, and heap roots remain open under T005/T006/T015/T027. No Android device run was performed.

### SEC065-FIDO-012 — Connected paired-host display name remained replay-cached after collection stopped

`Fido2HomeViewModel.connectedDeviceDisplayName` combines paired-device aliases and Android device names into a `StateFlow<String?>` consumed by the connected-state dashboard title. The new test reproduced the retention: after collector cancellation and the timeout, `Synthetic alias marker` remained instead of `null`. `Fido2HomeScreen` retained collected connection state, connected display name and paired-device aliases in Compose state while the Activity composition remained present in the background.
