### SEC065-VAULT-005 — Shallow generated copies shared erasable payload buffers

`PasswordPayload`, `SecureNotePayload`, `CreditCardPayload`, and `CustomField` were data classes with mutable `CharArray` fields. Their generated `copy()` methods shared every unchanged array and nested custom field with the source instance. Clearing either instance therefore erased the other's data. This also affected editor code that replaced one custom-field name/value with `current.copy(...)`, because the unchanged half remained aliased. The test-first `PayloadCopyOwnershipTest` failed both before the correction (two focused test failures). These types are now regular classes with explicit copies: defaulted fields and nested custom fields are deep-copied; arrays/lists explicitly supplied as copy arguments transfer to the result. Existing custom equality, hash code, redacted string representation, and `copyForEditing()` behavior remain. No in-repository destructuring/componentN use was found. The full `:feature:vault:testDebugUnitTest :feature:vault:ktlintCheck` suite passes 93 tests across 21 reports with zero failures, errors, or skips.

This addresses app-owned copy aliases only. Explicitly supplied replacement buffers remain caller-to-result ownership transfers; Compose snapshots/IME strings, crypto provider copies, and heap roots remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015/T027.


### SEC065-CTAP-015 — CTAPHID channel keys and parsed-message owner cleanup

`HidReportParser`, `HidChannelRegistry`, and `CtapHidRequestOwner` used lowercase hex `String` values as map keys for four-byte CTAPHID channel IDs. The parser could retain such a key for a partial message; the channel registry retained keys for the session; an active request owner retained its key for the request lifetime. Parsed `CtapHidMessage` copies also owned a channel array, but terminal cleanup cleared payload arrays inconsistently. Channel keys now use a packed `Int`; parser partial state retains no copied channel array, and the parser clears its temporary CID copy unless ownership transfers into a completed message. `CtapHidMessage.clearMemory()` clears both channel and payload arrays. Dispatch rejection and synchronous command paths clear the message, request admission failures clear after error handling, and admitted request owners clear only at completion. The U2F-to-CTAP2 asynchronous handoff copies the CID so the outer message can be erased safely.

The focused parser (19), request-owner (4), dispatcher privacy (4), and session-generation (1) classes pass; the full FIDO host suite passes 1,250 tests across 167 reports with zero failures/errors/skips; KtLint passes. The transport lifecycle test snapshots the CID at response-construction time because production now erases the message array immediately after synchronous framing.

This addresses app-owned map keys and message arrays. OS/runtime copies, channel bytes in already-built transport packets, provider/native retention, physical Bluetooth behavior, and heap roots remain open under T005/T006/T015/T027/T029/T034/T035.

### T005/T012 FIDO generated SQLDelight row signature review (2026-11-15)

Current generated SQLDelight row signatures were compared with `Fido2Database.sq` and the three sensitive projection schemas. BLOB-backed RP/user fields and lookup/encrypted metadata are mutable `ByteArray` properties; TEXT-backed credential IDs, AAGUID/public-key encodings, and optional consent device/IP/user-agent fields are immutable `String` properties. Credential and RP DAO bind buffers clear in `finally`; consent RP and lookup-token buffers clear in `finally`; `EntityMappers` clears mutable row arrays, and repository flow owners clear rejected/terminal rows. The generated row types are data classes with shallow array copies, but no production row-copy call was found; test fixtures do copy rows. See `evidence/sql-delight-generated-row-signatures.md`.

This is source/build-output mapping only. SQLDelight driver/native copies, SQLite page/WAL/journal cleanup, backup behavior, current release at-rest settings, immutable consent TEXT retention and heap roots remain open. T005/T006/T012/T015/T039 remain open; generated signature inspection is not runtime proof.

**SEC065-BLE-001 follow-up (2026-11-15):** `BleNotificationWriterTest` now directly checks that the writer-owned GATT send buffer is zeroed after the send callback returns, while the callback first observes the expected synthetic packet and the caller-owned input remains unchanged. The focused test and full FIDO host suite (1,252 tests across 167 XML reports, zero failures/errors/skips) pass with KtLint. Evidence is in `evidence/ble-notification-send-buffer-erasure.md`; Android framework/controller copies, device heap roots and physical transport remain open.

### SEC065-CORE-025 — Independent registration response credential-ID ownership

`AuthenticatorData` previously held the exact mutable credential-ID array owned by the persisted `PasskeyCredential`. Consequently, the documented `MakeCredentialResult.eraseSensitiveText()` cleanup corrupted the response's authenticatorData. The credential-ID alias regression failed before correction and passes after the response receives an independent credential-ID copy. Two additional regressions showed attestation arrays survived persistence failure and result cleanup; both failed before correction and now pass. `AttestationObject.eraseSensitiveData()` and `MakeCredentialResult.eraseSensitiveData()` clear the full mutable response. The correction also stages independent AAGUID, flags and RP-hash arrays, builds authData into one exact-sized buffer rather than retaining a `ByteArrayOutputStream` backing copy, owns an independent ClientData challenge copy, and clears staged buffers on signing rejection, failed construction and cancellation. The 42-case registration class and full 1,254-test/167-report FIDO host suite pass; KtLint passes. Provider/transport copies, caller lifetime and heap roots remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015/T027. See `evidence/registration-attestation-independent-credential-id.md`.


### SEC065-CORE-026 — Dead SecureMemory helper and active mnemonic buffer mapping

Repository-wide current-source search found no production or test caller of `SecureMemory`; the dead helper was removed. The live mnemonic normalization owner is `ErasableCharBuffer`, whose growth clears replaced arrays and whose terminal erase clears full capacity; existing host tests inspect real backing storage. `SecurityModule` composition reachability and the public BIP39 vocabulary resource loader are separately mapped. This finding does not establish provider/JVM copies, caller/IME strings, heap roots, or platform runtime behavior. T005/T006/T012/T015 remain open. See `evidence/secure-memory-and-mnemonic-buffer-review.md`.


**SEC065-CORE-026 scope extension:** Mapped common HDK/seed/provider interfaces as secret ownership contracts and retained the synthetic deterministic reference fixture with its provenance and historical-review references. The fixture has no current test consumer; its presence is not reported as a passing runtime test. The static unused `KeyWrappingPolicy` constant is assessed as no sensitive-data handling.


### SEC065-CORE-046 — Secret-derived HDK generic hash representation

`HdkKeyPair.hashCode()` and `HdkChild.hashCode()` previously incorporated their private scalar/child factor bytes. Neither class overrode `toString()`, so inherited generic formatting included the corresponding value-derived hash. This disclosed a deterministic 32-bit fingerprint rather than the raw secret; a live logging/formatting sink was not established in the inspected source references, so impact is conditionally R2. Both classes now return a fixed hash and fixed redacted generic label; equality semantics are unchanged and remain non-constant-time. No regression test was added or run for this source correction. Search and test remaining collection/hash callers, and continue caller, provider and heap-owner review under T006/T012/T015.

Mapped `PublicKeyDecoder` and its generated-key host tests under `CLS-FIDO-001` as credential-bound public metadata. The production decoder handles Base64/DER public-key data, not a private key; test methods generate synthetic keypairs and retain provider private-key objects without explicit destruction. This source-only review was not a test run and does not establish provider, runtime or heap lifetimes. See `evidence/public-key-decoder-owner-scope.md`.


### SEC065-CORE-028 — Remove obsolete credential-parameter salt storage

The unused `PublicKeyCredentialParameters` salt field and predictable `getSafeSalt()` helper were removed after current production-call-site search found no use. The new absence regression failed before removal and passes after it; full FIDO host suite and KtLint pass.

Mapped GetAssertion options, PRF input, RP identity and authenticator selection owners/tests to their current decoded-request cleanup path. The handler's request-map cleanup is the terminal owner for decoded PRF bytes. Full consumer/lifecycle/provider/heap review remains open; this is not a claim that all request aliases are closed.

Observer assertions verify exact contents while borrowed and zeroing after return; source inputs and returned HID packet copies remain valid. `SensitiveCredentialText.toUserId()` had no callers and materialized an immutable String-backed `UserId` from the mutable owner. ### SEC065-CORE-030 — UserId generic diagnostic redaction

`UserId` wraps a String-backed passkey account identifier and previously inherited a `toString()` that disclosed that value. A synthetic-marker test failed before correction. The value class now returns fixed `UserId([redacted])`; explicit `.value` access and storage/protocol behavior are unchanged. The common domain suite passes 92 tests across 16 reports and KtLint passes; the complete FIDO Android host suite also passes 1,261 tests across 167 reports and FIDO KtLint passes. Explicit value consumers, configured diagnostic capture, Kotlin/Native behavior and heap/provider lifetime remain open. See `evidence/userid-diagnostic-redaction.md`.

### SEC065-CORE-031 — RpId generic diagnostic redaction

`RpId` is a value class over a relying-party identifier classified as a sensitive association. Its default `toString()` exposed the raw immutable value to generic object formatting. A synthetic-marker regression failed before correction. The value class now returns the fixed representation `RpId(<redacted>)`; explicit `.value` reads and JSON serialization retain the original protocol value. The full core-domain Android host suite passes 95 tests across 18 reports with zero failures/errors/skips, and `:core:domain:ktlintCheck` passes. Configured diagnostic destinations, Kotlin/Native behavior, immutable String lifetime and heap/provider retention remain open. See `evidence/rpid-diagnostic-redaction.md`.

### SEC065-CORE-032 — DomainException generic diagnostic redaction

`DomainException.ValidationError` and `DomainException.RepositoryError` are data classes, so generated `toString()` included their public error fields, messages and (for repository failures) cause object. The synthetic-marker regression failed before correction. Both now return fixed type labels from `toString()` while preserving explicitly accessible `field`, `message` and `cause` properties. The full core-domain Android host suite passes 96 tests across 19 reports with zero failures/errors/skips, and KtLint passes. Current validator construction uses fixed strings and no in-repository `RepositoryError` constructor was found; direct property consumers, external callers, configured exception sinks and cause-object retention remain open. See `evidence/domain-exception-diagnostic-redaction.md`.

### SEC065-CORE-033 — Secure random failure output was accepted without validation

The iOS `generateSecureRandomBytes` actual ignored the `OSStatus` from `SecRandomCopyBytes` and returned its output buffer even when Apple reports failure. The platform call now runs through a shared cleanup boundary that returns the buffer only for status zero; nonzero status or a provider exception erases partial bytes and throws a fixed cause-free `IllegalStateException`. Android provider exceptions use the same boundary, and `CredentialId.generate` already clears a successfully encoded random byte owner in `finally`. The red-before synthetic status regression failed before correction; the three-case status/provider/success suite and full core-domain host suite pass (99 tests / 20 reports), and KtLint passes. Apple documents that the call returns `errSecSuccess` or another result and that callers must check it ([`SecRandomCopyBytes`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/secrandomcopybytes%28_%3A_%3A_%3A%29?language=objc)). iOS compilation/runtime and platform-provider behavior remain unverified on this Windows host. See `evidence/secure-random-platform-failure-cleanup.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-016 — Vault aggregate retry exhaustion retained the raw append failure

`VaultAggregateServiceImpl.execute` retained the last failed append `Result` across retries and returned its original exception after exhaustion. That exception can retain database details and an arbitrary throwable object graph. A synthetic marker/cause regression failed before correction. Retry exhaustion now returns a fixed `IllegalStateException` with no cause; command/event ownership cleanup remains unchanged. The focused aggregate class passes 14 tests and `:core:data:ktlintCheck` passes. Earlier `getState` failures and other repository exception translations, configured destinations, provider/native state, and heap roots remain open. See `evidence/vault-aggregate-retry-error-cause-discard.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-017 — Vault event repository retained raw persistence exceptions

`EventStoreRepositoryImpl` returned caught `SQLException` and `SerializationException` instances directly from append and read operations. These failures could retain database/parser messages and throwable graphs and were passed through aggregate state-read failures. The synthetic parser-marker regression failed before correction; SQL, serialization and other operation exceptions now map to fixed cause-free errors, while coroutine cancellation continues to rethrow. Focused source/host coverage and full module verification are linked in `evidence/vault-event-repository-cause-discard.md`. Snapshot repository failures, other unchecked provider failures, configured destinations and heap/provider lifetime remain open.

### SEC065-ERR-018 — Vault snapshot repository returned raw operation failures

Snapshot save/load caught only SQL exceptions in some paths and allowed serialization/provider exceptions to escape directly. These paths could retain exception messages and throwable graphs across the caller boundary. Two synthetic-marker regressions failed before correction. Snapshot operations now rethrow coroutine cancellation and map SQL, serialization and other `Exception` failures to fixed cause-free results; existing `finally` cleanup remains active for acquired key/plaintext owners. Focused and full `core:data` host evidence is in `evidence/vault-snapshot-repository-cause-discard.md`. Native/JDBC behavior, configured diagnostic destinations and heap lifetime remain open.

### SEC065-ERR-019 — Passkey aggregate propagated raw repository failures

`PasskeyAggregateServiceImpl` returned raw event-replay failures, retained the last append exception through retries, returned snapshot-save failures unchanged, and could return repository-thrown exceptions from command execution. A synthetic retry-exhaustion marker regression failed before correction. The aggregate now maps replay, snapshot, retry-exhaustion and unexpected command-execution failures to fixed cause-free errors while preserving cancellation propagation and existing mutable-owner cleanup. Full FIDO host-suite and KtLint evidence is in `evidence/passkey-aggregate-error-cause-discard.md`. Native/JDBC providers, configured diagnostics, provider-internal state and heap roots remain open.

### SEC065-ERR-020 — Vault aggregate propagated raw event-retrieval and thrown repository failures

`VaultAggregateServiceImpl.getState` returned raw full-history and snapshot-suffix replay failures, while thrown errors from event-store, append or snapshot operations could escape command execution directly. Synthetic read, snapshot-replay, thrown append and thrown snapshot regressions failed before correction. Retrieval and unexpected command failures now return fixed cause-free results; cancellation continues to propagate and existing snapshot fallback and owner cleanup remain in place. Full `core:data` host and KtLint evidence is in `evidence/vault-aggregate-retrieval-error-cause-discard.md`. Provider/native internals, diagnostic sinks and heap roots remain open.

### SEC065-ERR-021 — Credential ID decoder retained the raw Base64URL failure cause

`CredentialId.decodeAndValidate` returned a fixed malformed-input message but attached the decoder's `IllegalArgumentException` as the cause. A synthetic invalid-input regression failed before correction on the cause-absence assertion. The decoder now discards the lower-level exception and exposes only fixed validation text. `:core:domain:allTests :core:domain:ktlintCheck` passes 93 host tests across 16 reports, with zero failures/errors/skips; `:core:domain:detekt` passes; and the downstream FIDO host suite passes 1,265 tests across 167 reports with KtLint. Downstream formatting, native/provider behavior, immutable encoded ID lifetime, configured destinations and heap roots remain open. Evidence: `evidence/credential-id-decoder-cause-discard.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-022 — Registration challenge decoder retained the raw Base64 failure cause

`MakeCredentialOptions.withDecodedBase64Challenge` returned a fixed malformed-input message but attached the platform Base64 decoder exception as its cause. The synthetic malformed-challenge regression failed before correction because the cause was non-null. The decoder now discards it. Full FIDO host tests pass 1,266 cases across 167 reports; KtLint and Detekt pass. Native/provider behavior, downstream diagnostic destinations, immutable challenge Strings and heap roots remain open. Evidence: `evidence/make-credential-challenge-cause-discard.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-023 — Relying Party repository retained raw DAO failures

The save/delete/count-update `Result` wrapper returned ordinary DAO throwables directly and caught all `Throwable`s. A marker regression across all three methods failed before correction because the synthetic DAO message remained in the returned failure. The wrapper now maps `Exception` to a fixed cause-free `IllegalStateException`, continues to rethrow coroutine cancellation, and lets fatal `Error`s propagate. The six-case focused repository class passes; the full FIDO host suite passes 1,267 tests across 167 reports, and KtLint/Detekt pass. Provider details, production caller reachability, diagnostic destinations and heap roots remain open. Evidence: `evidence/relying-party-repository-error-cause-discard.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-024 — Passkey storage adapter retained raw operation failures

`captureStorageResult` returned persistence, encryption, serialization and conversion exceptions directly in `Result.failure`, retaining arbitrary messages and cause graphs before errors reached the already-sanitized aggregate boundary. The synthetic marker regression failed before correction. The adapter now returns a fixed cause-free failure for ordinary exceptions, preserves cancellation propagation, and lets fatal `Error`s propagate. Focused event-storage and replay suites pass 18 and 4 cases; the full FIDO host suite passes 1,267 tests across 167 reports, with KtLint and Detekt. SQLite/provider internals, direct caller reachability, diagnostic destinations and heap lifetime remain open. Evidence: `evidence/passkey-storage-error-cause-discard.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-025 — Transport preference results retained raw I/O failures

`Fido2SettingsRepositoryImpl` retained arbitrary `IOException` objects inside read/write result variants. Red-before tests showed both the suspending read and write outcomes preserved the original failures. Read/write errors are now cause-free singleton results across observed flow, lookup and save paths; current UI/process consumers continue mapping them to fixed status codes. The focused repository, settings ViewModel and process recovery suites pass 11, 9 and 4 cases; the full FIDO host suite passes 1,268 tests across 167 reports with zero failures/errors/skips; KtLint and Detekt pass. Other settings storage paths, configured destinations, provider behavior and heap retention remain open. Evidence: `evidence/transport-preference-error-cause-removal.md`.



### SEC065-ERR-026 — Shared error wrappers converted fatal Errors into ordinary outcomes

Both shared wrappers discarded throwable details but caught every non-cancellation `Throwable`, so fatal JVM `Error` instances were converted into ordinary domain failures. Red-before regressions failed for both helpers: `functionalCatching` wrapped an `AssertionError`, and `runCatchingOutcome` called its mapper. Both now rethrow coroutine cancellation, sanitize ordinary exceptions, and let fatal `Error`s propagate unchanged. The four-case privacy class and complete Core Common host suite (47 tests across 9 reports), KtLint and Detekt pass. The complete downstream FIDO host suite passes 1,268 tests across 167 reports, including the seven-case `PublicKeyDecoderTest`, with FIDO KtLint and Detekt. Native/provider behavior, configured destinations, heap roots and frozen-candidate acceptance remain open. Evidence: `evidence/functional-catching-fatal-error-propagation.md`.

### SEC065-ERR-027 — Optimistic concurrency throwable message disclosed aggregate metadata

`OptimisticConcurrencyException` interpolated its aggregate identifier and sequence number into the default throwable message and accepted arbitrary caller-provided message text. Because throwable formatting may reach diagnostics, a repository implementation throwing this exception could disclose the aggregate identifier. A red-before synthetic-marker regression failed; the message is now fixed and the arbitrary message parameter is removed while structured conflict properties remain available to retry logic. The full domain Android host suite passes 94 tests across 17 reports, with KtLint passing. A source search found no current constructor call site; the type and event-store contract describe its intended failure path, so the fix protects the defined contract without claiming an observed live throw. Configured diagnostic sinks, property misuse by callers, provider/heap lifetime and frozen-candidate acceptance remain open. See `evidence/optimistic-concurrency-error-message-redaction.md`.


### SEC065-VAULT-006 — Successful save dropped the decrypted editor copy without erasing it

`VaultNavGraph` kept an independent `copyForEditing()` payload in Compose state. On successful save it popped the editor and assigned all three edit-copy state holders to null without calling `clearMemory()`. Unlike cancel and graph disposal, this terminal path left the copied password, card, note and custom-field arrays populated until garbage collection. `clearEditingPayloadCopies` now erases each present payload and nested custom field; successful save invokes it before releasing the state holders, and each editor route invokes it on disposal. Failed saves retain the edit copy for retry. The synthetic owner regression verifies all field arrays are zero after repeated cleanup. The complete Vault unit suite passes 94 tests across 22 reports; KtLint and Detekt pass. The final API 35 navigation rerun passes all 15 cases. One earlier full run had a system-back timeout while the same cases passed isolated; after removing redundant Compose state mutation during route disposal, the complete rerun passed. Compose/IME, heap roots, provider copies and frozen-candidate evidence remain open. Evidence: `evidence/vault-editing-payload-terminal-cleanup.md`.

### SEC065-VAULT-007 — Vault list item metadata remained owned while the app was backgrounded

`VaultViewModel.state.items` retains mutable title, encrypted payload and CRDT arrays while the main shell remains alive. Before correction, those records were cleared only on replacement or permanent ViewModel disposal, so an app background transition could retain them. A query already in flight could also complete after cleanup and repopulate state. Label names remained in `state.labels` as immutable Strings. The graph now submits `ClearListData` on lifecycle `ON_STOP`, releasing item and label state references, then reloads the current label filter and labels after `ON_START`. Generation guards reject stale item and label queries, and zero stale mutable item arrays. Host regressions cover explicit clearing and deferred responses after background cleanup. Full Vault host tests pass 96/22 reports; KtLint and Detekt pass. API 35 lifecycle instrumentation passes the new focused case; the full navigation class still has two unrelated system-back timeouts. Compose title/label snapshots, heap roots and frozen-candidate behavior remain open. Evidence: `evidence/vault-list-background-owner.md`.

### SEC065-VAULT-008 — Editor drafts remained active when the app moved to the background

Editor field arrays and the independent edit-session payload were normally erased when the editor route was disposed. Backgrounding left the Vault graph composed, so those owners and a pending save could persist while the app was stopped. The lifecycle `ON_STOP` path now clears list/detail owners, abandons the pending mutation, zeros edit-session copies, clears label selection, closes the add-type sheet and returns navigation to the list. Route disposal runs the editor's existing field-buffer cleanup; foreground return reloads list/label data without restoring the draft. Full Vault host suite passes 96 tests across 22 reports with KtLint and Detekt. The isolated API 35 emulator editor-background test passes one case. IME/provider, renderer/accessibility, heap and frozen-candidate behavior remain open. Evidence: `evidence/vault-editor-background-session.md`.

### SEC065-DB-013 — Dormant passkey-label update API retained an unused persistence path

The custom-label update use case had no production caller, and the credential-details UI explicitly removed the custom-label field. The production FIDO database provider uses `EncryptedDriverFactory` and SQLite3MultipleCiphers, so a `TEXT` column alone did not prove plaintext at rest. Removed the dead update use case, repository API/implementation, DAO method and SQLDelight update query. Kept the legacy column/model field so existing values remain preserved by migrations; this does not claim those legacy values have been purged from historical database pages or heap. The full FIDO host suite (1,273 tests/168 reports, zero failures/errors/skips), KtLint and Detekt pass; the database migration case still preserves `legacy-label`. Verification hashes and limitations are recorded in `evidence/dormant-passkey-label-update-removal.md`. No production custom-label mutation or visible UI flow remains in the reviewed tree.
### SEC065-LOG-051 — MakeCredential logs mislabeled credential-ID length

The success path emitted `credId=${credentialId.size}bytes`; it exposed only the size, not credential bytes, but used an identifier-shaped field in logs. Removed this diagnostic and extended the existing captured-registration test to assert `credId=` never appears on success, malformed input, storage failure, or cancellation. Output-length and attestation-format diagnostics remain separate bounded protocol metadata and require destination-level matrix coverage under T029/T034/T035. The full FIDO host suite passes 1,273 tests/168 reports with zero failures/errors/skips, KtLint and Detekt pass, and the 24-case handler validation class report hash is recorded in `evidence/make-credential-identifier-diagnostic-removal.md`.

### SEC065-LOG-052 — Final local crash-log output must omit exception and tag details

The existing writer tests covered message redaction and the `PrivacyScrubbingLogWriter` forwarding wrapper, but lacked an assertion over final file content when a throwable is supplied. Added a temporary-file regression with synthetic marker text in the throwable message, cause, type, stack-frame fields and caller tag; the final file must retain only the operation message and generic throwable placeholder. The full FIDO host suite passes 1,274 tests/168 reports with zero failures/errors/skips, KtLint and Detekt pass, and `LocalCrashReportingLogWriterTest` passes all 12 cases; the XML report hash is recorded in `evidence/local-crash-log-final-output-privacy.md`. The test exercises the writer directly; configured `Fido2Initializer` ordering, Android app-private storage, Logcat, release retention and device failure paths remain open under T029/T034/T035.

### SEC065-LOG-053 — FIDO writers were installed after asynchronous transport startup

`ChimaliApplication.onCreate` launched `ProcessTransportSelection.startSnapshot()` before calling `Fido2Initializer.init`. The snapshot runs asynchronously on `Dispatchers.IO` and can emit Kermit diagnostics for preference-read failure or timeout; those messages could therefore race writer registration and reach Kermit's default writer. Writer initialization now occurs after DI setup and before `startSnapshot()`. App unit tests pass (7 tests across 3 reports, zero failures/errors/skips) and app KtLint passes. This is a source-order correction; no deterministic runtime scheduling test forced the race, and DI-construction output, final destinations and release/device behavior remain open under T029/T034/T035. Evidence: `evidence/fido-writer-startup-order.md`.


### SEC065-CORE-034 — Event-sourcing generic representations included caller-supplied sensitive values

`Snapshot<T>` data-class formatting rendered aggregate identifiers and materialized state, and `TraceEntry` formatting rendered caller-provided description and metadata. Both generic representations now redact those values; explicit properties, equality, serialization and persistence contracts are unchanged. Synthetic marker tests cover the diagnostic boundary and explicit-access compatibility. The full core-domain host suite passes 101 tests across 22 reports and KtLint passes. The current source search found no production `TraceEntry` constructor call site. Explicit property logging by external callers, serialization sinks and heap/runtime behavior remain open. Evidence: `evidence/event-sourcing-representation-redaction.md`.

Red-before regressions failed for both event families; the full core-domain host suite passes 103 tests/24 reports with zero failures/errors and KtLint passes. Explicit-property sink review, external implementations, persistence/provider copies and heap roots remain open. Evidence: `evidence/event-diagnostic-redaction.md`.

The unused in-repository `feature.vault.model.CustomField` data class had generated `toString()` output that included its caller-provided immutable name. The service now clears the array in `finally` until ownership transfers to the staged item. The complete `VaultCryptoOwnershipTest` class and KtLint passed, and a focused green rerun passed. ### SEC065-FIDO-014 — Credential management ViewModel retained identity owners after its screen stopped

`CredentialManagementViewModel` retained its full credential list, selected/delete-dialog credentials, pending-removal owners and mutable search query when `CredentialListScreen` stopped. The screen now lifecycle-bounds state/effect/removal collection, clears local search and biometric prompt references, and signals stop/start. On stop the ViewModel invalidates request generations, cancels search, empties state and pending-removal bookkeeping without committing deletion, ignores delayed screen intents such as a biometric callback, and erases all unique mutable credential and query owners. On start it reloads credentials. The `CredentialManagementViewModelTest` suite passes 29 cases including a synthetic pending-removal stop/erase/resume/reload regression that also verifies stale ConfirmDelete/CommitDelete intents are ignored; FIDO KtLint passes. XML SHA-256: `66dc78df2aa397e38bbfdd836cb6f834dbb515b119dc53c8f0f05add3bb50fa4`. BND-UI-009 records the Compose/biometric text boundary. This verifies mutable ViewModel ownership only; renderer, accessibility, biometric API immutable snapshots, DB/provider copies, physical UI and heap behavior remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015/T027.


### SEC065-FIDO-015 — Home-screen observer retained active request objects in background Compose state

`Fido2EventObserver` collected the event bus's pending registration and authentication StateFlows with plain `collectAsState()`. Because the Activity composition may remain while stopped, the Compose observer retained request objects containing credential/RP/user context and continued reacting to new requests while backgrounded. The observer now uses lifecycle-aware collection that resets its local request references to `null` on stop. `Fido2UiEventBus` remains the owner of an active request until its deferred operation completes; the request is re-observed on resume if still pending, preserving the operation. `LifecycleClearedStateTest` and `PairedDevicesSectionLifecycleTest` pass together on API 35 (`OK (2 tests)`); the lifecycle test verifies that the source StateFlow retains its value while the UI reference resets and delivers current state after restart. The test uses generic synthetic text, not a real CTAP request; no actual event-bus request, system host, renderer, accessibility or heap behavior was exercised. See `evidence/fido-home-event-request-lifecycle.md` and BND-UI-010. Request model, event-bus, transport/provider, deferred callback and heap ownership remain open under T005/T006/T012/T015/T027.

### SEC065-FIDO-019 — process transport address transfer

`ProcessTransportSelection` owns a process-scoped active transport and borrows any caller Bluetooth address only for a supported Classic HID connection. Invalid/unresolved selection and BLE outbound selection reject without forwarding. The controller does not persist or log the address. `disconnect` releases the active transport and collector; `close` cancels the collector only, so platform transport/device-reference lifetime remains a separate owner. The classification register and inventory now include this transfer path.


## SEC065-PLATFORM-001 — Camera permission present in release manifest

**Finding:** The app main manifest and FIDO Android main manifest contributed `android.permission.CAMERA` and an optional camera hardware feature to the release merge even though CameraX is used for the development QR scanner. That scanner processes a 24-word seed QR value, so production packaging must not advertise camera access for this debug-only tool.

**Disposition:** Remediated in the exploratory worktree by moving the permission and optional feature declaration to `app/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` and removing them from both main manifests. `:app:processReleaseMainManifest`, `:app:processDebugMainManifest`, and `:app:processClipboardAuditMainManifest` passed; the release and clipboardAudit merged manifests lack the camera permission and hardware-feature declaration, while debug includes both. `:app:assembleRelease` compiled and completed lint analysis but was manually interrupted during `minifyReleaseWithR8`; no full release assembly pass is claimed. Evidence: `evidence/release-camera-permission-scope.md`.

**Remaining limits:** This is source/variant packaging evidence from a dirty worktree, not frozen-candidate evidence. Runtime scanner behavior, other build variants, third-party ML Kit temporary data, immutable camera/QR `String` copies, and heap lifetime remain separate unresolved obligations under T005/T006/T015/T029/T037.


## SEC065-KEY-QR-001 — Development mnemonic QR scanner owner path

The debug development scanner receives ML Kit `Barcode.rawValue` as an immutable `String`, counts it, copies a trimmed 24-word value to a mutable `CharArray`, and transfers that array through `DevelopmentToolsScreen` to `DevToolsViewModel` and the seed-import provider. The ViewModel terminal and completion cleanup covers the transferred array, including a job that never starts. Scanner disposal closes its scanner/executor and unbinds CameraX; the frame is closed for null images and normal asynchronous completion.

**Disposition:** Owner path and test gaps are recorded in `evidence/mnemonic-qr-scanner-owner-review.md`; inventory usage records map the scanner and UI separately. BND-UI-003 does not authorize the CameraX/ML Kit provider `String`. Synchronous image/input/`scanner.process`/listener setup failures now close the frame through `withMnemonicFrameRelease`; three host cases cover synchronous failure, asynchronous/double completion, and failure after completion registration. The owned-frame cleanup path is verified at this source boundary. ML Kit's immutable `rawValue`, internal/provider copies, camera/native frame copies and runtime disposal remain unresolved. Dev Tools navigation is gated by the Android debuggable flag. The available merged release manifest artifact dated 2026-11-16 has no CAMERA permission; this is not a rebuilt or frozen-candidate result. The scanner is evidenced as debug-build reachable, while future/frozen-candidate reachability remains subject to T036. Assess whether this development tool is required, then either remove the feature or strengthen its remaining ownership/test boundary. T005/T006/T012/T015/T027/T037 remain open.


## SEC065-PLATFORM-002 — Bluetooth adapter wait-state diagnostic

`BluetoothAdapterStateWaiter` reads only the adapter-state integer, bounds startup polling to ten 500 ms retries, and emits a fixed failure message without a caught `SecurityException` or its details. The existing marker-capture test verifies the `ERROR` fallback and no throwable forwarding. Source and test owners are mapped under `CLS-ERR-001` in the inventory. The full FIDO host suite passed 1,279/169 with no failures/errors/skips and KtLint passed. Configured production destinations and device behavior remain open; this does not certify Bluetooth runtime behavior.


## SEC065-FIDO-BLE-001 — BLE framing payload-owner cleanup

`FidoBleFraming` borrows input frames, allocates independent protocol frames, and owns partial reassembly bytes until reset or successful `FeedResult.Complete` transfer. Reset and framing errors zero the partial buffer; `BleSessionOwner` resets it for new initialization, empty/malformed data and timeout. New regressions verify reset zeroing, invalid-sequence zeroing, unchanged input frames, and complete-payload survival after transfer. The full FIDO host suite passed 1,282 tests/169 reports with KtLint.

This does not close downstream queue/transport cleanup, Android Bluetooth framework copies, provider/native heap state, or physical BLE behavior; those remain separate open owners. See `evidence/fido-ble-framing-payload-owner.md`.

### SEC065-CORE-041 — CTAP assertion response retained account owners after encoding

The successful assertion response path erased authenticator data and signature but retained the owned `PublicKeyCredentialUserEntity` mutable ID/name/display-name buffers. `encodeResponse` also left its local response map populated with the account text fields after the CBOR encoder returned. A regression that retained the actual serializer map failed before correction because the map remained populated. The response finalizer now erases the assertion's user owner on successful or failed encoding, and the encoder clears its response map in `finally`. Success and synthetic CBOR-encoder failure regressions verify zeroed user fields and map-reference release; all 19 `Ctap2GetAssertionHandlerTest` cases and FIDO KtLint passed in the original correction. A 2026-11-17 follow-up then found that response-map construction itself materialized immutable `String` copies through `user.id.value`, `user.name` and `user.displayName`. The response path now adds caller-owned mutable text projections, encodes them directly through the existing `SensitiveCredentialText` CBOR support, clears the empty-display-name temporary immediately, and erases every retained projection in `finally`. A new red-before assertion captured non-`SensitiveCredentialText` map values; its updated green test verifies protocol field content while encoding and zeroing after both success and synthetic encoder failure. The focused 19-case suite and FIDO KtLint pass; the complete FIDO Android host suite passes 1,311 tests across 172 reports with zero failures, errors or skips. CBOR output remains a live protocol response, and provider/transport copies and heap roots remain outside this host evidence. See `evidence/assertion-response-account-owner-cleanup.md`.

The transferred `RawCtapResult` remains live protocol output by design; CBOR encoder/provider copies and transport/heap behavior remain open. Evidence: `evidence/make-credential-response-cbor-temporary-cleanup.md`.

### SEC065-DIAG-ROTATION-FALLBACK-001 — Isolated diagnostic audit startup and rotation threshold

The disposable clipboardAudit flavor previously started the full ChimaliApplication, which repeatedly exceeded the emulator's process-start window during instrumentation launch. It now uses ClipboardAuditApplication to install only ClipboardModule, preserving the dedicated target activity's service while avoiding unrelated production startup in this audit flavor. The rotation test's synthetic padding was scrubbed before the size check, so a 256-byte limit did not rotate the file; the test now uses 64 bytes, below fixed record overhead. On API 35, the method-filtered test passes (1 test): both active and backup logs are redacted with fixed throwable wording, and a blocked log directory emits only fixed fallback text without marker/path/exception content. Test output and APK hashes are retained under vidence/diagnostic-file-rotation-fallback-api35-success.md. This closes only the controlled audit-variant writer case; release behavior and the full diagnostic matrix remain open under T029/T034/T035.

All redaction, active/backup rotation and blocked-path fallback assertions passed (1 test); stdout and APK hashes are retained in the evidence index. ### T016 prior US1 coverage snapshot (2026-11-17)

Reconciled the current `source-inventory.json` snapshot against the US1 task labels and finding crosswalk. US1 coverage is defined here by the owner requirements assigned in its tasks: FR-003/004/005/010/011/015. The snapshot contains 726 file records and 633 usage records overall; 515 usages across 474 distinct files carry at least one of those US1 requirements. Of those US1 rows, 447 reference a finding, covering 191 distinct `SEC065-*` identifiers. All 191 have a task/story disposition: 169 are named directly in `tasks.md` and 22 are assigned by the T007 crosswalk. Across the complete inventory, all 232 distinct `SEC065-*` finding IDs are accounted for by either a direct task reference or the crosswalk. This verifies identifier coverage, not completion of each owner or evidence criterion.

A fresh status-field scan finds 304 of the 516 current US1 rows explicitly use an unresolved-status term such as “open”, “unverified”, “remaining”, “unknown” or “not established”. This lexical scan flags rows for review; it does not distinguish a resolved code fix with a separate open platform boundary from an unresolved application owner. The exact usage IDs, source paths, finding IDs, evidence IDs and full owner status text remain in `source-inventory.json`; flagged rows must be dispositioned individually under T006/T012. **T015 partial heap inspection (2026-11-17):** Shark 2.14 parsed the restricted API 35 baseline/active/terminal HPROFs from the isolated synthetic mnemonic owner. The active mnemonic `char[]` was rooted through the Activity `ViewModelStore` and `DevToolsViewModel` state; no matching String or `char[]` was found in the terminal snapshot. Two exact matching byte arrays remained in the dump without a GC-root path and have unknown origin; no forced GC was used. Positive-control String and backing byte array were rooted at all checkpoints. This supports only the scoped mnemonic path and does not establish absence of allocations or physical erasure. At that partial-capture stage, vault, recovery/derivation and identifying passkey groups and additional terminal paths remained unverified. Later captures and the T015 completion audit are summarized below; provider/native/platform roots and release-equivalent behavior remain Unverified. Detailed results: `evidence/heap-capture-mnemonic-api35-20261117.md`.

**T015 partial passkey identity heap inspection (2026-11-17):** A synthetic credential retained by the production `CredentialManagementViewModel` was captured at baseline, active and post-Activity-disposal checkpoints on the isolated API 35 target. Shark rooted account name/display name, RP ID and user ID arrays, encoded credential-ID String/backing bytes, and the credential-ID array through `ViewModelStore → CredentialManagementViewModel.fullCredentialList → PasskeyCredential`. Mutable arrays were directly asserted zeroed on disposal; the positive control remained rooted throughout. The terminal LeakCanary assertion may trigger collection; any matching objects without root paths have unknown origin and do not prove physical erasure. See `evidence/heap-capture-passkey-api35-20261117.md`. This is one synthetic management-list item, not a broad passkey, provider, native or release-equivalent result. At that interim stage, vault and recovery/derivation groups had not yet been captured; see the later T015 completion audit.

**T015 partial wallet recovery/derivation heap inspection (2026-11-17):** A synthetic generated mnemonic and provider initialization were captured at baseline, derivation, active, activity-disposed and post-clear terminal checkpoints on the isolated API 35 target. Shark attributed active mnemonic ownership to the Activity ViewModelStore/`DevToolsViewModel`; active PRK and parent private-scalar arrays were rooted through `WalletMasterSeedProvider.cachedPrk` and `cachedParent.privateKey`. The test retained a session owner through active capture and asserted cache arrays zeroed and references released after `clearSession()`. A matching derived-seed array was found at the derivation checkpoint without a GC-root path; that snapshot cannot attribute a coroutine local-stack owner. The positive-control String and backing bytes were rooted in all five snapshots. Matching unrooted arrays remain unknown dump artifacts and do not prove live retention or physical erasure. See `evidence/heap-capture-wallet-derivation-api35-20261117.md`. This covers one synthetic recovery/derivation path only; vault and other required groups are recorded in later entries. Broader failure/cancellation paths, other passkey flows and provider/native/platform boundaries remain open outside the defined capture procedure.

Shark rooted note title/content and nested custom-field arrays through `VaultState.selectedSecureNotePayload`, and stored title/payload/CRDT arrays through `VaultState.selectedItem`. Activity disposal zeroed every mutable owner asserted by the instrumentation; disposed/terminal HPROFs contain no matching secret-array GC-root paths. ### SEC065-CTAP-006/007 follow-up — CBOR map alias review (2026-11-17)

The current decoder creates independent byte-string arrays, mutable text owners, and fresh list/map containers. Sensitive map-key owners are compared only with the fixed request-key allow-list and erased immediately; unknown-key values are erased and omitted. Partial decode failures erase all codec-registered owners before return. Both request handlers erase successful decoded values in their terminal `finally` paths. Source review found no parser-created alias that allows a decoded mutable value to appear at multiple map/list positions. The current `CborCodecTest` report passes 18 cases, including mutable owner transfer/erasure, map-key and unknown-value cleanup, HMAC salt cleanup, and partial-owner cleanup after an injected non-format failure. This closes only parser-created aliasing; handler-level joined borrowers beyond the awaited HMAC call, provider/native copies, and heap roots remain open. See `evidence/cbor-decoder-map-alias-review.md`.

## T007 disclosure-risk ranking — Bluetooth diagnostics (partial)

**Ranking basis:** R1 denotes direct exposure of authentication secrets, recovery material, private keys, or decrypted secret values; R2 denotes identifying device/account metadata or exception details that can reveal or link a user/device; R3 denotes non-identifying operational metadata. Rank reflects the pre-correction source behavior and reachable event, not proof of a configured sink. The five findings below are R2 because their former values included peer addresses or platform exception/state text; no credential or recovery-secret path was identified in the reviewed emissions.

| Finding | Exact reachable source and trigger | Pre-correction impact / priority | Current source disposition | Residual exposure question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC065-LOG-008 | `feature/fido2/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/fido2/data/transport/BleSessionOwner.kt`; a second host connection is attempted while another host owns the session. | Attempted and active peer MAC addresses were emitted on rejection, linking nearby paired-device identities; R2. | Fixed rejection label; focused synthetic-address capture confirms omission and preserves the first host session. | Configured Logcat/file/crash destinations and other BLE emissions remain under T029/T034/T035. |
| SEC065-LOG-009 | `feature/fido2/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/fido2/data/transport/BleGattTransport.kt`; an inactive notification or stale disconnect callback arrives after a successor session connects. | Stale peer MAC address was emitted, exposing/linking a paired device; R2. | Fixed callback labels; synthetic stale-callback regression confirms omission and successor-session continuity. | Configured destinations and additional callback paths remain under T029/T034/T035. |
| SEC065-LOG-010 | `feature/fido2/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/fido2/data/transport/BluetoothHidTransportImpl.kt`; connect/disconnect/state/report lifecycle and failure callbacks. | Peer identity, exception text, state error text, and attached throwables could disclose linkable device or platform context; R2. | Fixed labels without throwable forwarding; synthetic-address lifecycle regression and suite evidence recorded. | Error-result callers and configured diagnostic destinations remain open under T029/T034/T035. |
| SEC065-LOG-011 | `feature/fido2/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/fido2/bluetooth/BluetoothHidReportSender.kt`; Android report send returns failure or throws. | Platform exception text/throwable could expose device or implementation context; R2. | Fixed failure label; red-before host capture and caller-buffer preservation regression passed after correction. | Configured destination output remains unverified under T029/T034. |
| SEC065-LOG-012 | `feature/fido2/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/fido2/bluetooth/BluetoothHidDeviceWrapper.kt`; Android rejects a supplied Bluetooth address during connection. | Invalid-address details and throwable were forwarded, potentially disclosing caller-supplied device identity or platform context; R2. | Fixed label without throwable forwarding; synthetic invalid-address capture passed and failure remains closed. | Configured destination output and adjacent wrapper failure paths remain open under T029/T034/T035. |

This bounded ranking does not replace the rest of the current 246-ID inventory reconciliation, full diagnostic reachability/destination matrix, or frozen-candidate review; T007/T029/T034/T035/T036 remain open.

|---|---|---|---|---|

A later ViewModel/item disposal could therefore leave multiple decrypted notes, passwords or card fields reachable in app-owned arrays; R1. | `clearMemory()` now zeros title/payload/CRDT arrays. Detail loading transfers only the selected item and clears other or untransferred fetched records in `finally`. R1 because the data can be a decrypted private note/password/card payload; reachability is limited to allocation failure during synchronous batch replay. | The decoded event is now individually cleared if `owned.add(event)` fails; earlier events remain cleared by the batch `finally`. | Source correction only. No regression was added or run, and the existing later-row failure test does not exercise failed list admission. Caller release, serialized/provider copies, heap roots and frozen-candidate behavior remain open. |

## T007 wallet parent-key copy admission risk ranking (partial; 2026-11-18)

R1 is used because the orphaned intermediate is a P-256 private scalar capable of signing authenticator assertions. Reachability is limited to allocation or construction failure after the scalar copy is allocated and before ownership transfers; this is not evidence of a routine-path leak.

| Finding | Exact source and trigger | Pre-correction impact / priority | Current source disposition | Residual exposure question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC065-SEED-003 | `WalletMasterSeedProvider.initializeLocked` copies `parentResult.privateScalar` before copying the public point into an `HdkKeyPair`; `getDeviceKeyPair` repeats this ordering from `cachedParent`. | If the public-point copy or wrapper construction failed after the private-scalar copy succeeded, outer cleanup erased only the original source scalar. The detached copy could remain populated until collection; R1 conditional on allocation/construction failure. | Both call paths use `copyOwnedParentKeyPair`, which owns the scalar copy until successful construction and erases it in `finally` on failure. | No allocation-failure regression or run was added. Provider/JCA copies, caller terminal erasure, heap roots and frozen-candidate behavior remain open under T006/T012/T015/T036. |
| SEC065-SEED-004 | `Bip39MasterSeedGenerator.entropyToMnemonic` allocated a digest before its index array and populated a secret phrase before return. | If index allocation failed, the digest had no cleanup owner; if word copying failed, the partially written phrase had no cleanup path. Conditional R1 on allocation/copy failure; no routine-path failure is inferred. | Digest, index and phrase arrays are staged under `try/finally`; all failed exits clear them, and the phrase transfers only after successful population. | No allocation/copy-failure regression was added or run. Caller cleanup, ICU/JCA copies and heap roots remain open under T006/T012/T014/T015/T036. |

This source correction closes only the identified staging gap; it does not certify the provider's full secret lifetime.

## T007 mnemonic scanner secret-exposure ranking (partial; 2026-11-18)

R1 denotes recovery material whose disclosure can enable wallet compromise. The `DevelopmentToolsScreen` and scanner source exist in the Android main source set, but the available release evidence does not show a usable scanner path: Dev Tools navigation is gated by `isDebug`/`FLAG_DEBUGGABLE`, and the available merged release manifest artifact dated 2026-11-16 has no CAMERA permission. Treat this as a debug-build recovery-secret boundary for that inspected snapshot; reassess if a release route or camera permission is introduced.

| Finding | Exact reachable source and trigger | Impact / priority | Current source disposition | Residual exposure question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC065-KEY-QR-001 | `Fido2RegistrationNavGraph` includes `DevelopmentToolsScreen`; `AppNavGraph` exposes the Dev Tools item only when `isDebug` is true. The scanner receives ML Kit `Barcode.rawValue`, counts the phrase, copies a valid trimmed 24-word value to `CharArray`, then transfers it through the screen to `DevToolsViewModel` and the seed-import provider. | A valid QR can contain the complete wallet recovery phrase. In a debug-enabled build, ML Kit's immutable `String`, camera/frame/native buffers, or provider copies could retain the phrase beyond the app-owned mutable handoff; disclosure would permit wallet recovery; R1 for that debug path. The release path is not established as scanner-reachable. | App-owned phrase arrays transfer to the importer and clear on completion, rejection, cancellation, disposal and never-started work. Null-image, synchronous image/input/scan/listener setup failures and asynchronous completion all close the `ImageProxy` exactly once; three host regressions cover those close paths. Dev Tools navigation uses `isDebug`, whose Android implementation reads `FLAG_DEBUGGABLE`; the available merged release manifest artifact from 2026-11-16 has no CAMERA permission. | ML Kit `rawValue` String and native/provider copies remain opaque; device scanner disposal and heap behavior are unverified. The menu gate is current source evidence; the manifest is an older build artifact and neither is frozen-candidate proof. Reassess release reachability if navigation gating or merged permissions change; decide whether to keep the debug-only scanner or further bound provider copies (T005/T006/T012/T015/T027/T036/T037). |

This ranks the possible recovery-phrase exposure; it is not evidence that the scanner is reachable in a release build or that ML Kit retains the value.

## T007 clipboard disclosure and integrity risk ranking (partial)

The R1/R2/R3 scale above applies. R2 covers the period during which a user-requested secret is available in a shared platform clipboard and the possibility that best-effort cleanup cannot run; it does not imply that the user-requested copy itself is an unauthorized app action. R3 covers the documented non-atomic race that can affect later clipboard availability but does not disclose the copied secret to another party.

| Finding | Exact reachable source and trigger | Impact / priority | Current source disposition | Residual exposure or verification gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC065-CLIP-ANDROID-001 | `core/common/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/core/clipboard/AndroidClipboardManagerService.kt`; a user invokes password copy, then an eligible delayed cleanup or ownership-unknown/replaced/failure outcome occurs. | The password is placed on the Android clipboard by request. Another eligible clipboard reader may observe it during the exposure window; app suspension/process death, inaccessible ownership metadata, or a rejected platform clear can extend that window beyond 30 seconds; R2. | Source sets `ClipDescription.EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE`, installs ownership metadata before publication, schedules a fixed 30,000 ms best-effort attempt, checks metadata without reading clip text, and reports bounded outcomes. Blind clearing is not attempted. | API 29 foreground/app-switch/process-death results exist, but API 28/30–32/33–34/35+ physical cohorts, lock/sleep, physical preview/TalkBack, external replacement race, full app-copy interleavings and frozen candidate remain open under T027/T036/T037. |
| SEC065-CLIP-ANDROID-002 | `core/common/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/chimali/core/clipboard/AndroidClipboardManagerService.kt`; any caller supplies an identifying clip label while copying a password. | A caller-controlled label could expose account/RP identity in clipboard metadata or previews; R2. | Caller labels are ignored; the published label is the fixed non-identifying `Password` value. The synthetic-label regression and 29-case host class pass. | Physical OEM/API preview behavior and release-candidate presentation remain open under T027/T036/T037. |
| SEC065-CLIP-IOS-001 | `core/common/src/iosMain/kotlin/com/chimali/core/clipboard/IosClipboardManagerService.kt`; user-requested password copy and scheduled pasteboard cleanup. | The password is present in the shared pasteboard; cancellation, app suspension, process termination, or unavailable ownership state may leave it available longer than intended; R2. | The adapter uses pasteboard change-count metadata and bounded receipts, avoids reading clipboard text for ownership, and skips clearing when ownership is uncertain. | Kotlin/Native compilation was skipped on this Windows host; iOS runtime, pasteboard preview/history behavior, process interruption and frozen-candidate verification remain open. |
| SEC065-CLIP-IOS-002 | Same iOS adapter; scheduled cleanup delay is cancelled by interruption, supersede or explicit manual clear. | Before correction, an interrupted current job could leave a pending receipt/ownership record without a terminal result, obscuring that cleanup was interrupted while the password remained on the shared pasteboard; R2. | Current-job interruption records `Interrupted`; intentional supersede/manual-clear removes the ownership guard before cancelling the job. | No iOS build/runtime result is available; cancellation, UIKit copies and observable removal remain Unverified. |
| SEC065-CLIP-IOS-003 | Same iOS adapter; coroutine dispatch begins after the successful pasteboard publication. | Starting a fresh 30-second delay after dispatch could push the cleanup attempt later than the recorded deadline and extend password clipboard exposure; R2. | Delay is now computed from monotonic publication time and uses only the remaining time; a late-dispatched attempt becomes immediately eligible. | KtLint passes, but Kotlin/Native compile and iOS execution remain Unverified; this is a scheduling correction, not a removal guarantee. |
| Clipboard replacement check/clear race (SEC065-CLIP-ANDROID-001 / SEC065-CLIP-IOS-001) | Platform clipboard changes after the readable ownership check but before the clear request is applied. | A competing app's later clip could be erased; this is an accepted non-atomic availability/integrity race, R3, not evidence of secret disclosure by the app. | Both adapters check ownership before an automatic clear and skip when ownership is different or unreadable. | Platform atomic compare-and-clear is unavailable in the reviewed APIs; retain the race explicitly in T027/T039 and avoid any perfect-preservation claim. |

These entries prioritize the known clipboard risks but do not close T007/T027: complete physical cohort/case evidence and a frozen-candidate comparison remain required.

## Current disposition refresh — 2026-11-18

This refresh supersedes the older counts and test totals in the implementation row under “Implementation and release dispositions.” The current exploratory inventory has 731 file records (575 Relevant and 156 assessed-no-relevant-usage, zero Open file dispositions) and 639 unique usage records. These are separate counts. A prior structural audit found all paths present and hashes matching, with unique usage IDs, reciprocal file/usage links, and a usage row for every Relevant file; the latest added UI owner links were also accepted by the evidence validator.
